## 404, layout b on phones — the first scroll is held for the number — 2 October 2026

- **Why (author, on a phone):** "Try a slide-in scroll on mobile for the b option… the fill animation is a bit too abrupt, and the problem on mobile is that it scrolls to the footer, and I want it first scroll the fill / 404 slide in."
- **The problem, measured (402 × 874):** the page scrolls 297px and the number leaves with the footer, so the scroll that fills the number is the scroll that takes it away; a flick crosses the whole range in about 0.2 s and the level snapped up behind it (a 400 ms catch-up).
- **Built** (`not-found.css` → *the first scroll is the number's*; `src/js/not-found.js`) — first behind `?nf=b&fx=slide`, then, the same day, **the default for `?nf=b` (author: "Let's make this interaction the default for this option, please. I need to test on a real device")**; the switch and its body class are deleted: on narrow screens (≤ 991) **the first 400px of scroll are the number's alone.** The page holds still — `#page` is sticky inside a body made 400px taller, the browser's own pin, no script in the scroll path — while the 404 **slides up out of the footer**, from 0.45em under the edge (its tops showing: 50px of 141 at 402) to its place, **and fills**; then the page scrolls on to the footer as before. The slide rides Gentle and the level the house duration (800 ms, was 400), so a flick that crosses the hold in one go still lands as one move (sampled: the scroll done at 0.3 s, the number home and full at 0.6 s). No reveal animation and no idling level in this mode — the scroll brings it in. A page shorter than the screen (an upright iPad) holds for the whole of its scroll. Scrolled back to the top, it sinks and empties.
- **Reduced motion:** no hold; the number in its place, filled. **Scripts off** (`@media (scripting: enabled)` gates the low start): layout b as it was — the number in its place, the level idling.
- **Said plainly:** this is the pinned scene called "the deliberately wrong one" in the entry below — it holds a guest on the one page they most want to leave. What makes it worth the trial here: it is one swipe long, the page answers the thumb the whole time, and the buttons stay on screen and work throughout.
- **Checked** (headless Chrome with touch, the local site; touch drags and a 700px flick): 402 × 874 RU, 375 × 667 EN, 820 × 1180; reduced motion; the desktop unchanged; no sideways scroll — re-run on the plain `?nf=b` address after the switch was removed. **Not checked — and it matters here: a real iPhone.** A sticky element under an `overflow-x: clip` ancestor trembled on iOS in September; here the clip is on the pinned element itself, not above it, which should be safe, but only a device says. Also unseen: Safari's toolbar collapsing during the hold.
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → *404 page — on trial*; `testing.md` → 404 page.

## Dream Team wall strip — a mouse drags it — 2 October 2026

- **Why (author, from testing):** on a desktop with a mouse, "discovering that you need to use a wheel for this is very difficult" — the strip should answer a drag. The wheel → sideways of 27 Sep (the entry below) stays; the drag is added beside it.
- **Built (`src/js/about-drift.js` → Drag, `src/css/about.css` → after `.about-team__drift`):** press and pull the strip sideways with the mouse; let go while still moving and it glides on and slows (speed from the last 100 ms of the pull, 6% lost per 16 ms frame, stops at either end; a pause before letting go throws nothing; no glide under reduced motion). **Mouse only** — touch and pen keep the browser's own scroll, untouched. **A press that travels under 6px is still a click**, so a print still opens the lightbox; a real pull swallows the click it ends in (a capture listener on the strip, before the print's own). A new press, or the wheel, stops a glide. The photos can't be dragged out as ghost images; the strip's labels can't be text-selected (pointer: fine).
- **Cursor:** the open hand (`grab`) on the strip's ground — the gaps, the line, the "To be continued…" card; the closed hand (`grabbing`) everywhere on the strip while pulling. **The prints keep the pointing hand** (author, 1 Oct: a print promises a click — *Team wall lightbox*), so the grab shows only between them. 🔶 If testers still miss the drag, the alternative is `grab` over the prints as well, the click still opening the lightbox — the author's call.
- **Checked in headless Chrome at 1440 × 900 (private server, this branch's bundle):** a 300px slow pull moved the strip 300px, `is-dragging` + `grabbing` while pulling, no glide after a pause, no lightbox; a quick 200px throw glided 390px further, no lightbox; a pull back past the start stopped at 0; a click with 3px of jitter opened the lightbox; no console errors. Owed: a real mouse in Safari and Firefox, and a trackpad (its own two-finger swipe is untouched; a click-and-drag on a trackpad now pulls the strip).


## 404, layout b — the scroll carries the number; the Russian line — 2 October 2026

- **Why (author):** "What if we add a parallax effect to the B option, at least on desktop, so the 404 slides in from a partially [covered] state to the middle of the page and becomes fully filled (it can be a different position / fill level)? Right now, my problem with the desktop is that it feels unfinished; it feels like you have to do something with this page but don't get how. On mobile, I just don't see how the parallax can work (just on letter fill, probably?)."
- **Read against the brief:** the feeling has two causes in Motion language. *Idle self-demo, human seizure* — the level rising and falling for ever is an idle demo, and an idle demo advertises an interaction; this page has none, so it asks for a touch it can't answer. *Outline → fill is the state change* — "filled" means arrived, and a fill that never stays never arrives. And every other page has a page-load entrance; the 404 just appears. So: the number arrives once and ends filled.
- **The scroll, on a wide screen — first refused, then built (the author: "can you make it arrive on scroll rather than on the page reveal?"; then "it can be the default position or your parallax option on page reveal").** The page scrolls only **135–172px** there (1440 × 900 / 1280 × 800). Claude's first answer was that the theater leaves with the scroll, so the middle could only be reached on load — **wrong**: that reasoning assumed ~320px; with 135–172 the theater stays in view, and at the scroll's end the number stands in the middle of its column with the footer under it. What the short range does cost: one wheel notch (100px) is 58–74% of the whole move, so the number rides the scroll on the house spring instead of being pinned to it.
- **Two answers were built behind a switch, `?nf=b&fx=rise|stand`, and the author chose** (`inc/not-found.php`, `404.php`, `not-found.css` → *layout b answers the scroll*, new `src/js/not-found.js` registered in `main.js`):
  - **rise — by the scroll, the author's parallax. Kept.** The page opens as layout b: the number comes up to its place in the footer on the reveal (from 0.45em under the edge to 0.1em, an outline) and the level idles as before — an idle demo that now advertises something real. **The scroll takes over at its first pixel** (`--nf-p`, 0 → 1 over the page's scroll): on wide screens (≥ 992) the number climbs out of the footer to **the middle of its column** — its centre on the copy's centre at the end, measured 0px apart at 1280, 1440 and 1920 — and the level fills as it goes; it follows on Gentle (800 ms), so a wheel's notch lands as one move in about half a second. Scrolled back, it sinks and empties and the idle resumes. Narrower screens, where the number stays in the flow above the footer: the level alone follows the scroll (402 × 874: a third at 100px, two thirds at 200, full at the page's end, 297). A page that doesn't scroll (an upright iPad) shows the end.
  - **stand — on the reveal, set aside.** Every screen: the number came up from 0.45em to its place in the footer and the level climbed to the top, once. A solid Chili 404 standing in the Lime.
  - **Decided (author, the same day): "The scroll+parallax works better for this option… make scroll+parallax a default for this option please, some of my participants already have the links."** `?nf=b` is the scroll version now; `stand`, the `fx` switch (`sweet_pepper_not_found_fx()`, `data-fx`) and its keyframes are deleted, so a link with `&fx=…` shows the same page. `?nf=bt` keeps the number in the footer with the idling level. Without the script layout b idles as it did.
  - **Reduced motion:** no entrance, no idle, no link to the scroll — the number in the middle of its column, filled.
- **Left to watch:** the 135–172px of scroll on a desktop — the move is over in one or two wheel notches; and phones, where at rest the page is the outline and the idling level as before, and the fill waits for a scroll.
- **The deliberately wrong one, not built:** pin the first screen for one screen of scroll while the number climbs and fills — a real parallax scene. It breaks the service-page rule (the Visit entrance: everything readable inside a second; a 404 is the most service page of all) and makes the guest scroll through a show to reach the footer's links. Not worth the exception here.
- **The Russian line (author):** «На кухне искали везде.» → **«Искали и на кухне, и в подсобке, и за баром.»**; «…и мы подадим что-нибудь хорошее.» → **«…и закажите что-нибудь вкусное!»** (`data/not-found.php` → `sauce`, layouts 0 and b). The first sentence also opens layout a's line («…Всё остальное — в меню.») — carried there too, say if a should keep the old one. It sets in two lines on a desktop (0, a, b), three on a 402 phone in 0 and b, three in bt. English unchanged.
- **Checked** (headless Chrome on the local site, sampled every frame, after the switch was removed): `?nf=b` with real wheel notches at 1280 × 800, 1440 × 900 EN and 1920 × 1080 — rest (the level idling), one notch (58–74% of the way, landed in ~0.5 s), the end (the number's centre on the copy's, 0px apart; filled), back to the top (home, idling again); by scroll position at 402 × 874; reduced motion; `?nf=bt` unchanged; the script blocked — layout b as it was; no sideways scroll. **Not checked:** Safari, a real phone.
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → *404 page — on trial*; `testing.md` → 404 page.

## 404 page, layout b — the number keeps the row's 48px from the copy and stands higher — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author, in the browser's inspector on `?nf=b`):** "there is no 48px gap in container not-found__inner, although I see that the font size includes it in the formula." Then, on the Russian page after a first fix: "now it looks even negative."
- **Found — two things.** (1) `.not-found__inner` declares `gap: 48px`, but on wide screens the number is `position: absolute` — out of the flow, and a gap only separates items in it. Its place came from its size formula alone, which counted the 400px copy column and **10px**: the box stood **12px** from the column at every width from 1024 to 1920. (2) **The column was not what the eye sees in Russian:** the headline was allowed to run out of it (to 548, so «В СТОП-ЛИСТЕ.» would not break at its hyphen) on the reasoning that it sits above the digits' tops — so its second line ended **64px past the number's left edge**. Counting 48 from the column (the first fix) only cut that to 28: still negative, as the author saw.
- **Built (`src/css/not-found.css`):** the column is as wide as the headline's longest line needs — **`--nf-col`, typed per language** (400 EN, where GOT LOST IN is 394; **480 RU**, «В СТОП-ЛИСТЕ.» 476), the Location headline's method — and the headline no longer leaves it. The number's size counts the column and the gap: `(min(100vw, 1280px) − 2 · gutter − --nf-col − 48px) / 1.834` (1.834em: the set's box, 1.876, less the last digit's tracking, which a negative margin pulls back).
- **Measured, 992 / 1024 / 1163 / 1280 / 1672 / 1920, RU and EN:** number box → column 48.0px; number → the widest line seen 54 (EN) and 52 (RU). Headline lines as before (EN 3, RU 2). **The cost:** the number is smaller — EN Molot 366 at 1280 and up (was 386), 262 at 1024 (was 282); **RU 323 and 218** — the Russian headline takes 80px more of the row. Below 992 nothing changes (the number is in the flow there). Layouts a and bt untouched.
- **To retune:** `--nf-col` when a headline changes — the Russian one is still a draft (brief → 404 page → open).
- **The number stands higher: 0.1em under the footer's edge, was 0.16 (`--nf-sink`).** The author, leaning to b ("a better hierarchy, especially on mobile"), tried 0.04em in the inspector and was not sure. Five depths were shot on the live page, desktop and phone, at rest and with the level up (`Claude outputs/nf-sink/sink-sheet.png`, close-ups in `feet.png`). **The depth is decided by the glyphs:** the 4's bar ends 0.13em above the foot, the 0's counter 0.16em, and the 0's corners begin to round 0.08em up. So 0.16 (the old value) laid the counter's stroke on the Lime edge and 0.13 the bar's; under 0.08 the 0 is cut through its curve — at 0.04 it reads as a cropping slip, and the number no longer stands *in* the footer but on it, which is the layout's idea (lost in the sauce, the level rising). **0.1em:** the bar clears the edge, only straight strokes go under; 194px of the number seen at 1280 RU (was 174), 121 on a 402 phone (was 109). **Decided (author, on the local site): "Ok, let's use 0.1em."**
- **The level follows the depth:** at rest the Chili starts 15px under the edge, below the 12px bites, at any size (`inset(calc(100% − --nf-sink + 15px) …)`). The fixed 83% it had left 4px of Chili showing in the bites on a phone at rest, and would have shown a band above the edge at any shallower depth — what the author saw when moving the number in the inspector.
- **No way back up on the 404 (author, the same evening — «Наверх» crossed out by hand in a phone screenshot; asked, the answer: "it doesn't make sense to keep Back to top or ru version on a page without scroll").** `footer.php` leaves both links out when `is_404()` — the phones' «Наверх» / Top beside the name and the desktop bar's «Наверх» / Back to top — in every layout and both languages; not printed rather than hidden, so nothing dead is left for a keyboard or a screen reader. The desktop bar keeps the copyright at the left and the legal links move to the right end; the phone row is the name alone. Every other page keeps both links (checked: `/`, `/en/menu/food/`).
- **Still open:** which layout — the author is between 0 and b.
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → *404 page — on trial*; `testing.md` → 404 page.

## Page loader's replay — its own pace: «Подано!» at 3.3 s, not 7 — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** "check the page loader (the easter egg one that opens from the footer) overall speed; it feels too slow, especially on mobile, and it doesn't match the website's energetic tone."
- **Found (sampled per frame at 402 × 874 with touch and at 1440 × 900 — the same clock on both):** the replay borrowed the real wait's creep, `0.9 · (1 − e^(−t/3 s))` — a curve built to *never arrive*, because a real wait has no known end. A replay knows its end, and sat through it anyway: lunch at 1.0 s, **half the track at 2.45 s, then four seconds for the next 29%** (party at 5.4 s), «Подано!» started at 6.5 s, the controls came at **7.0 s**. The knob's speed fell to **a ninth** of its opening speed (30% of the track per second → 3.4%).
  - **Why worse on a phone:** (1) the column is 338px against 825 — the same clock is 101 → 12 px/s there, a knob that looks stalled; (2) the poster heats a word at a time — SWEET by 1.4 s, PEPPER by 4.05 s, and **BAR, the biggest line on the screen, was 39% hot when the creep ended at 6.5 s**; (3) the × waited for «Подано!» with the other controls and a phone has no Esc — **seven seconds with no way out** unless the guest happened to touch the slider.
  - **The Figma prototype is twice as fast** (`page-loader-heat` 2729:72296, read from its reactions): the knob is at the middle in 0.3 s (ease-out), rests 0.8 s, reaches the end 2 s later — **3.1 s** — and the page takes over 0.8 s after. The build was the slow one, not the drawing (the lesson of the hover pass, again).
  - **Not slow, left alone:** the open fade (95% in 0.13 s), the line swap (0.18 + 0.18 s), the pop (0.42 s), the letters (40 ms apart), the controls' entrance (Gentle), the idle hints' 4 s clock, the real wait between pages.
- **Built** (`src/js/page-loader.js` → `PACES`, `startShow()`; `src/css/page-loader.css`): **the replay has its own curve** — the same creep, steeper: `1.266 · (1 − e^(−t/2.8 s))`, 0.8 of the track in 2.8 s, then the run to the end as before. Lunch 0.63 s · dinner 1.42 s · party 2.52 s · «Подано!» from 2.8 s · **the end and the shaker at 3.3 s**. The knob never drops under 37% of its opening speed (phone: 153 → 56 px/s). A line every 1.2 s — three, the last one up 0.2 s before «Подано!» (they were 1.2 s, then 2.2 s apart). On the phone poster SWEET is hot at 0.86 s, PEPPER at 2.1 s, BAR with the end. A shake replays at the same pace (back on «Подано!» 3.6 s after the tap, was 7.3). Reduced motion: «Подано!» after 2.8 s (was 3).
  - **The × is there from the start** (`.has-exit`, on Gentle, on screen by 0.3 s) — on every screen. The shaker and «Вернуться на сайт» still arrive with «Подано!» or at the first touch: they are the reveal, the × is the way out. This changed the author's staging ("then × slides in"); kept with the pace.
  - **Three other paces went to the author behind a `?pace=` switch** (for the browser tab): **`slow`** — as it was, × at the end; **`figma`** — the prototype's own timing: the middle in 0.3 s, a rest, 2 s to the end, «Подано!» at 3.1 s (the first three dayparts pass in a blink; on the poster SWEET is hot at once); **`flash`** — the deliberately wrong one: the whole track in one Gentle spring, «Подано!» in half a second, the toy at 1 s. It breaks the loader's premise (a creep and the team's lines — one line per open), though every shake becomes a whoosh and a new line.
  - **Decided the same evening (author): "Let's keep the auto, please, it feels better."** The three other paces, the switch (`PACES`, `spLoaderPace`), the two easing helpers they needed and the docblock line are deleted; the pace is three constants in `page-loader.js` (`REPLAY_FOR` 2800, `REPLAY_CAP` 1.266, `REPLAY_LINE` 1200). `?pace=` now does nothing.
  - **A race fixed on the way:** the last 450 ms run to the end could not be stopped; closed during it, it ended with no replay to stay for and lifted the loader 650 ms later — a reopened one. It is on the loader's frame handle now, so a close (or a finger on the knob, which it used to fight) stops it. Reachable only by Esc before; with the × up from the start, by a tap.
- **Checked** (headless Chrome, the worktree's bundle served into the local site's page; the list re-run after the other paces were deleted): the four paces' timelines; a drag mid-show holds the knob (no run-on after 1.5 s); drag to the end serves; shake; ×; Esc and focus back on the footer's item; close → reopen within 0.4 s plays to the end; `?egg=0` lifts by itself at 4.2 s; reduced motion.
- **Not checked by Claude:** a real phone (the author's verdict came from their own look) — watch whether the long lines («Договариваемся с медведем с герба») read in 1.2 s.
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → Page loader → *Replay*; `testing.md` → Page loader → *The replay's pace*; `design.md` §9 (the prototype's timing); `inc/page-loader.php` (the switch list). Scripts: `Claude outputs/loader-pace/`.

## 404 page on phones — layout 0 as the Figma mobile frame — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author, an iPhone screenshot of sweetpepper.bar marked up by hand):** "the default one (with the jumping letters) is very messy" — notes "smaller" on the gap between the header and the digits and on the gap under them, a squiggle by the copy. The author drew the phone frame: "content" 2827:79374 and the digits `lettersSize-mobile` 2827:79143 (four states, the popped digit 200 against 180), "can be adjusted to match the actual viewport size".
- **Found:** the phones used the desktop set scaled down — Molot `min(238px, 47vw)` in a box of 1.483em, the room the desktop's × 1.176 swell needs. At 402 that left ~70px of empty box above and below the glyphs: 120px from the header to the digits, 95px from the digits to the notice line. The copy wrapped as one long line and a stub.
- **Built (`src/css/not-found.css`, ≤ 767 only; desktop and tablet unchanged):** Molot `min(180px, 44.78vw)` — 180 at 402, scaled with the screen; the set's box is its line (0.9em), since the phone swell is × 1.111 (`--nf-pop`, now a variable the keyframes read; 1.176 elsewhere) and fits inside the line's own air; neighbours step 0.0327em aside (half the growth); glyphs 23px apart at 180, as drawn. The theater: `clamp(24px, 5.5svh, 48px)` on top (Figma 48, less on a short screen), 48 under the buttons; the 26 · 26 · 24 · 40 gaps were already the frame's. The copy takes `text-wrap: balance`.
- **Checked** (headless Chrome, the worktree's theme on a private server; RU `/` and EN `/en/`): 402 × 874 at all four beats — header → glyph tops 67px (Figma 68), glyphs → notice line 42 (Figma 43), the copy in balanced lines (EN three lines as drawn, RU two), the buttons side by side; 375 × 667; 1440 × 900 unchanged (the set's box 353px as before); no sideways scroll. **Not checked:** a real phone, Safari.
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → *404 page — on trial*; `testing.md` → 404 page.

## Page loader's toy — the shaker beats on phones, the × on the house motion — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** "check the easing of the close icon and the shake it button on mobile in the page loader (the easter egg one that opens from the footer). The shake it button is still, while it should match the picker shake it button animation."
- **Found (a 402px touch screen, sampled per frame):** the shaker never moved while idle — it borrows the dish picker's markup, so a mouse gets its hover rattle, but the picker's idle beat (`.is-hinting`) is started by the picker's own script and nothing started it here. The × ran its own mix: a 0.5s expo-out slide, 0.3s fades, and a 0.3s Bouncy press that dipped past 0.92 and sprang back.
- **Built:** `src/js/page-loader.js`, `src/css/page-loader.css`.
  - **The shaker's beat, where nothing hovers:** the picker's own — swells 1.17×, rattles, glows by night (the glow keyed to the loader's mode, not the page's theme). **One clock, two hints in turn** (Motion language → One clock per section): the knob nudges, and 1.3 s later the shaker beats, every 4 s. A touch on the slider ends the knob's nudges and quiets the shaker for 8 s (the picker's seizure), then it beats alone; **the first shake ends it** for the page — the guest has found it. A mouse gets no beat: the hover state does that job, as in the picker.
  - **The ×:** slide-in, fade and tint on Gentle (`--dur-gentle`, the spring), like every other control since today; the press answers in 100 ms. The wide screens' «Вернуться на сайт» button comes in on the same.
- **Checked** in headless Chrome with touch emulated, on the private server and the local site: idle — knob at 0.5 / 4.5 / 8.5 s, shaker at 1.8 / 5.8 s (swell, rattle and night glow all running); after a tap on the track — no knob, the shaker again from 8 s; after a shake — neither; with a mouse at 1440 — the knob only, as before.
- **Not checked:** a real phone — whether two hints in turn read as "drag, or shake" or as too much.
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → Page loader → *the replay is a toy*; `testing.md` → *The footer replay as a toy*.

## Motion — the Gentle spring is the colour curve too; the hover trial is closed — 1 October 2026

- **The decision (author):** "Let's do `?ease=spring`, for everything." Earlier the same day: "I like Figma's gentle curve and timing, but I agree with the participant that the timing should balance the overall design… 250 feels like no easing for me" — and whatever won was to be the site's default, not a button rule.
- **What the measurement showed** (the entry below has the table): Figma's Gentle is half done at ~150ms and 95% at ~320ms; its 800ms is a settling tail. The site's colour curve, the "flat twin", had kept the 800 and lost the attack — half at 281ms, 95% at 581ms. The hovers ran at half the speed of the prototype they came from.
- **Built:** `src/css/variables.css` — **`--ease-gentle-flat` now holds the spring's bezier**, `cubic-bezier(0.33, 0.57, 0.08, 1.19)`, the same curve as `--ease-gentle`; 800ms unchanged. One line moves every declaration on the token (142: buttons, nav links, cards, chips, strokes, fades, the entrances' fades). The token's name stays — 140-odd rules use it, and colour can be re-pointed alone again.
  - **Sampled on the live Reserve button** (Chili → Chili Deep, per frame): 17% at 50ms · 35% at 100 · 76% at 200 · 94% at 300 · half at ~135ms · 95% at 317ms · peak 104% (three levels of red past the end for a moment — not visible; a channel already at its limit clamps). By the curve: half at 150ms, 95% at 323ms. *(The 152 / 325 quoted in the trial were the no-overshoot fit's — a hair slower; the spring was the one chosen.)*
  - **A new `--ease-flat`** (`0.4, 0, 0.2, 1`, the old twin) for nine declarations a spring would spoil — it eases every keyframe interval and overshoots each stop: the 404's looping hue and level (`not-found.css`), the shaker's rattle, swell and glow (`dish-picker.css`, `about.css`), the loader knob's hint (`page-loader.css`), and the lightbox print flying home (`team-lightbox.css` — "400ms, the flat curve" by its own spec).
  - **The trial is gone:** `src/js/hover-trial.js` deleted, its start removed from `main.js`, `--dur-hover` / `--ease-hover` removed and `.btn` back on `var(--dur-gentle) var(--ease-gentle-flat)`. `?hover=` and `?ease=` now do nothing.
- **Rule overruled, on purpose:** *Motion language → Colour-only transitions take the flat curve* (written Sep 2026 on the reasoning that an overshoot flickers on a colour) — rewritten there.
- **The mobile drawer joined, later the same day (author: "let's try the Gentle one on the drawer too").** `mobile-drawer.css` takes its timing from four `--drawer-*` properties, Gentle by default: the sheet fades over 800ms on the spring both ways (sampled: 23% at 100ms, 65% at 200, half at ~165ms, there by 400), the menu button's bars turn on `--ease-gentle` and overshoot to 46.6° before settling at 45 (−1.6° on the way back). **`?drawer=quick`** brings back the first build's 200ms in / 150ms out for the visit (sessionStorage `spDrawerTrial`, `html.drawer-quick`); `?drawer=gentle` clears it. **Found while slowing the fade:** on closing, the header dropped to its own layer at once and the fading sheet washed over it — the bar now keeps its raised layer for the length of the fade (`z-index` steps down after `--drawer-dur-out`), measured on top in every frame. The press stays 100ms.
- **Checked** in headless Chrome: the hover sampled on the private server and on the local site; home, About, Visit, the menu and `/en/` load; the 404 answers. **Not checked by eye:** the entrances (heroes on load) on the new curve — their fades start faster now; Safari.
- **Audited, not built — `Claude outputs/motion-audit/transition-audit.md`:** 63 declarations use none of the house curves. Proposed: **37 to match** (32 plain hover / state changes on 0.15–0.3s `ease`; 5 scrims and overlays, with `pointer-events` cut on close so an invisible scrim does not swallow clicks for the spring's tail), **23 to keep** (15 with their own choreography — the About Story's timeline and odometer on easeOutQuint, the reduced-motion crossfades, the rail's nudge; 8 whose clock is not ours — scroll-driven `linear`, the Visit marquee, the loader's timeout), **3 for the author** (the logo's back-ease on hover; the form fields' 0.15s focus keyline). *Considered and not built, at the author's question:* easeOutQuint at 800ms is the nearest no-overshoot neighbour (half at 104ms, 95% at 361 — it leaves at full speed, Gentle winds up for 50ms and overshoots); easeOutCubic at 800ms is half at 166 but 95% only at 506 — it would need ~500ms to finish with Gentle.
- **The match group, built (author: "let's match them"; the logo hover kept — "feels better than I have in Figma"):** 38 declarations in 14 stylesheets moved onto the tokens — colour, opacity and shadow to `var(--dur-gentle) var(--ease-gentle-flat)`, the three moves (the team chip's padding, the card's gap, the Copy chip's slide) to `var(--ease-gentle)`: About's quote link, milestone names, team chip and careers link; the contact form's field keyline and icon (hover, focus, valid, error); the Copy chip; the picker's tags, Shake It! and station grounds; footer and header links and icons; the home hero tiles' shadow, dim, keyline and sheets (the sheets' discrete `overflow` delay follows the new time); the nudge's ×; the page's ground on a theme change; highlight, privacy, vacancy and reserve-drawer links and ×. **The four scrims and the team dialog** (reserve drawer, section panel, "Write to the team") fade with their panels and carry `pointer-events: none` when closed — measured: 60ms after closing, a click at the scrim's place lands on the page, while the scrim is still 88% opaque. **Left as they were, on purpose (25):** the About Story's timeline and odometer (easeOutQuint) and its timed fades, the reduced-motion crossfades, the rail's nudge, the scroll-driven and looping `linear` animations, the loader's timeout, the logo's back-ease — and **the hero's language nudge (`.lang-nudge` opacity 0.3s)**, which the audit had under "match": its script collapses the row at the first `transitionend`, so a longer fade would hold the row open half a second after the nudge has gone. Checked: computed transitions on home, the menu and About; the reserve scrim's open / close / click-through; the kept ones untouched. Not checked by eye: the hero tiles' hover and the theme change at the new pace.
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → Motion language (*Colour takes the spring*, *Quick*), Platform → Test site (this session's steps: none beyond the pull and the rsync); `design.md` §6 (*Motion*) and §9; `style-guide.html` → 06 Layout, *Motion — easing* (on `main`); `styleGuide-report.md` §12; `testing.md` → *Mobile drawer and button hover speed*; the audit in `Claude outputs/motion-audit/`; the Motion table and the rows of every touched stylesheet below.

## Reserve drawer — the messenger links take the Secondary button's states, the call button the Lime primary's — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author, testing the desktop drawer):** "`btn-social btn-social-vk` … looks like it has the wrong states."
- **What was wrong (measured, desktop, both themes):** hover flooded the button **Lemon** with a Peppercorn label — by day and by night alike; there was **no pressed state** (a press looked like the hover); and the move ran `transition: all 0.2s`. The rule predates the button recipes: Lemon is the highlight, not a ground for an action (`design.md` §2), and the phone sheet already calls these links `ButtonSecondary-Dark`.
- **Built:** `template-parts/components/reserve-drawer.php` — both links (VK, Instagram) carry `btn btn-secondary` beside `btn-social`; `src/css/reserve-drawer.css` — `.btn-social` keeps only its shape (label left, arrow right, 14 / 16 padding, 8px radius, a 20px line so the button stays 50px tall), its own colour, hover and transition rules are deleted. Colours and states are `buttons.css`'s one rule now:
  - **night** (the sheet is Soft Peppercorn): Lime keyline and label at rest · hover the ground lifts to 25% Lime · pressed 15% Lime with the inset, 100ms — the label never changes;
  - **day** (Parchment): Olive keyline and label · hover Cream with a Lime keyline and a Peppercorn label · pressed the same with the inset.
  - The hover follows `--dur-hover`, so these links are on the `?hover=` trial with every other button.
- **Checked** in headless Chrome on the local site: rest, hover and pressed read from the computed styles, day and night; the button's box unchanged on desktop (328 × 50, 8px) and on the phone sheet (370 × 43.6, 4px, centred).
- **The call button, the same day (author, with Figma `ButtonPrimary-Green-Dark` 341:15618: "we also can have a primary lime button with the right and refined states").** `.btn-call` answered with `opacity: 0.9`, had no pressed state and ran its own `0.3s / 0.2s`. The frame: rest Lime + Peppercorn label · hover Cream + Ash label · pressed the hover's colours with the `Tapped-btn` inset — which is `buttons.css`'s `.btn-primary-green`, value for value. So the button carries `btn btn-primary-green` in both places it lives (`template-parts/components/reserve-drawer.php`, the copy button; `template-parts/home/contacts.php`, the phone block's `tel:` link) and `.btn-call` in `reserve-drawer.css` is shape only (the full row, no keyline — it keeps its 328 × 41.6 / 370 × 41.6 box).
  - **Open** (Lime): the frame's states. Both colours move on hover, and here that is safe — two light grounds, two dark labels, 11.8:1 falling to 7.2:1 with no crossing.
  - **Refined:** the Lime primary's press now takes 100ms (`.btn-primary-green:active`), as the Chili primary and the secondary — for every Lime primary on the site.
  - **Closed** (Chili + Lemon label): the Chili primary's states — hover and pressed deepen the ground to Chili Deep, the label stays (3.4 → 6.5:1).
  - **Busy** (orange `#E8751A` + white): **no frame draws its states — mine:** the same logic, the ground 15% toward Peppercorn (`#C8661A`), the label stays (3.0 → 3.9:1), the inset on a press. The author's to confirm or redraw.
  - **Copied** still wins while the pointer is on the button: Lime, «Номер скопирован», two seconds.
- **Not checked:** the Figma frame for the messenger link on desktop (none was given; the states are the documented Secondary recipe); the Instagram link (hidden here by `inc/geo.php`; same classes); busy and closed on the real clock — the three states were forced by class for the measurement; the Visit page's own call button (`.visit-cta__call`, phones only, already a Lime primary — its busy / closed grounds do not change on hover, the press shows the inset).
- **Still open:** white on the busy orange is 3.0:1 at rest — under AA for a 16px label (recorded since 20 Sep, `report.md` → *Call button, closed state*); neither orange is a palette token.
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → *Secondary (outline) button on a dark ground* (the links), *Lime primary — states* (new); `design.md` §9; the `buttons.css`, `reserve-drawer.css` and `reserve-drawer.php` rows below.

## Mobile drawer — the header stays, the hamburger turns into the ×; button hover speed on trial — 1 October 2026

- **Why (a tester, passed on by the author — "it makes a lot of sense"):** "the button hover effects should be a little faster — the whole design, colors, and style feel fast and energetic, but the hover animations feel a bit too slow. The same thing with the hamburger menu on mobile: the animation can be faster and cleaner. When we click the hamburger menu, the nav can stay fixed so we don't see any movement. Only the hamburger icon can change to the close button." The author: fix the drawer, try a faster button easing.
- **Measured on the old drawer (402px):** the sheet covered the header with its own copy of it, and the copy did not sit where the original does — the logo **8px to the right** (the sheet's 24px padding against the header's 16px gutter), the × 8px left of the hamburger and half its size (12px Ash against 24px Olive). The whole sheet also slid 8px down on the spring (overshooting 0.3px) while it faded over 800ms on the flat curve: **4% there at 100ms, half at 286ms, 90% at 520ms**. By day the Parchment bar with its rounded corners dissolved into the sheet's Paper.
- **Built — the drawer (default, no switch):** `header.php`, `template-parts/components/mobile-drawer.php`, `src/css/mobile-drawer.css`, `src/css/header.css`, `src/js/mobile-drawer.js`, `src/css/variables.css`.
  - **The header never moves.** The sheet opens *under* the site header: the header is raised over it while it is open (`html.drawer-open .site-header`, z 1001 against the sheet's 1000). The sheet's own header row — its logo link and its × — is gone from the markup.
  - **One button, two jobs.** The hamburger is drawn as three bars in CSS (the glyph's geometry: 24 long, 2.88 thick, round ends) so the same strokes cross into the × where they stand: the outer two meet in the middle and turn 45°, the middle one fades. The × is 17px across, in the hamburger's colour (Olive by day, Lemon by night) — the mockup's is 12px and Ash. Its name swaps between «Открыть меню сайта» and «Закрыть меню сайта» (both strings existed).
  - **Nothing slides.** The sheet fades in over 200ms and out over 150ms on a new **Quick** curve (below): 58% there at 55ms, 93% at 138ms. No transform.
  - **One edge.** The sheet's sides are the page gutter (16px on phones, the header's own) instead of a fixed 24, so the list, the card and the buttons start under the logo and the arrows end under the ×; rows are 16px wider on a phone. The list starts where it did, 100px down.
  - **No longer a dialog.** Its close control now lives outside it, so `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` are off the sheet: it is the menu button's disclosure (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). While open, everything but the header and the sheet is made `inert` by the script (main, footer, the reserve sheet, the notices) and put back on closing; Tab runs round the logo, the button and the sheet's links; Escape closes; focus stays on the button throughout. A floating language nudge (tablets, English pages) hides while the sheet is open.
- **New motion tier — Quick** (`variables.css`): `--dur-quick: 200ms`, `--ease-quick: cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.2, 1)` — for what *answers a tap*: it starts at full speed and lands softly. Gentle (800ms) stays for what enters, moves or sets a scene. This bends *Motion language → Colour-only transitions take the flat curve, at the house duration*; recorded there.
- **Built — the button hover, on trial:** `src/css/buttons.css` reads `--dur-hover` / `--ease-hover`, which default to the house 800ms on the flat curve — **nothing changes without the switch**. `src/js/hover-trial.js` (new, started from `main.js`): `?hover=400` · `?hover=250` · `?hover=150` (any 80–800) puts the buttons on that time and the Quick curve for the visit (sessionStorage `spHoverTrial`); `?hover=250flat` keeps the flat curve; `?hover=off` clears it. Why the curve matters as much as the time: at 800ms flat a button is **1% changed at 50ms, 4% at 100ms, half at 281ms**; 400 Quick is 36% · 58% · half at 80ms; 250 Quick is 50% · 76% · half at 50ms; 250 *flat* is still only 13% at 50ms. **Closed the same day — the spring won, for the whole site; see the entry above.**
- **The curve, not only the time (author, later the same day):** "I like Figma's gentle curve and timing, but I agree with the participant that the timing should balance the overall design. I'm between 400ms and trying another curve while keeping 800ms. 250 feels like no easing for me" — and whatever wins becomes the site's default, not a button rule. **Measured against Figma's own spring** (mass 1 / stiffness 100 / damping 15, solved, not the bezier): it is **half done at 147ms, 95% at 313ms**, passes 100% at ~380ms and spends the rest of the 800 settling a 2.8% overshoot. The site's colour curve — the "flat twin" (`--ease-gentle-flat`) — kept the 800 and lost the attack: **half at 281ms, 95% at 581ms**. So the site's hovers run at about half the speed of the Figma prototype they were taken from; the tester felt the twin, not Gentle. A no-overshoot fit of the spring, `cubic-bezier(0.44, 0.98, 0.07, 1)` at 800ms, stays within 2% of it (half at 152ms, 95% at 325ms). For comparison: 400ms flat — half at 141, 95% at 291, but 4% at 50ms; 400 Quick — half at 81; 250 Quick — half at 51 ("no easing"). **Second switch, in `hover-trial.js`:** `?ease=gentle` re-points `--ease-gentle-flat` to that fit **for the whole site** at the unchanged 800ms (138 declarations follow the token: buttons, nav links, cards, chips, fades); `?ease=expo` and `?ease=spring` (the overshooting original) for comparison; `?ease=off`. `?hover=` also takes a curve word now (`400gentle`, `400expo`, `400spring`). Checked: the token and the stored choice on buttons and nav links across pages, the two switches together, a nonsense value ignored. 34 transitions on the site are written without the token (plain `ease` or `all`) and follow neither switch.
- **Checked** in headless Chrome, RU and EN, on this branch's private server and then on the local site: the header's box, logo and button rects are identical closed and open (home day and night, About, Visit, the menu page at 768); the fade and the bars sampled per frame; the keyboard round and Escape; every `inert` put back; 360 × 640, where the sheet scrolls under the bar; the hover switch carried across four pages and cleared.
- **Not checked:** any real phone or Safari — the bars' sub-pixel thickness, the tap on the button while the sheet fades, VoiceOver's reading of the disclosure. The pressed state of the hamburger (it has none, as before).
- **Open:** the hover speed (`testing.md`); only `.btn` is on the switch — nav links, text links, chips and cards still answer over 800ms, and should follow the winner; the RU string «Меню сайта» (the old dialog's name) is no longer used and still sits in `languages/ru_RU.l10n.php`.
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → Mobile — the drawer (*Header*, *Opening and closing*), Mobile header, Motion language (*Quick*); `testing.md` → *Mobile drawer and button hover speed*; `design.md` §9; the file-table rows below.

## Connectors — word 35%, reflection 22% Avocado / 15% Lime, and an opacity lab — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author, from device tests):** "having no opacity on the section word ruins the hierarchy." First pass word 50 / reflection 30 with an Avocado day reflection; then the author asked for a test bench ("I don't see the opacity property in the dev mode") and tuned it there: day word 0.35 · reflection 0.22 · Avocado, night word 0.35 · reflection 0.15 · Lime — "at least this".
- **The lab:** `sweet-pepper-theme/lab/connector-opacity.html` (served at `/wp-content/themes/sweet-pepper-theme/lab/connector-opacity.html`, `noindex`, linked from nowhere): the home Highlights → Bar seam as two sections, the real SVG exports inlined so the ink swaps exactly; Day/Night, Desktop/Phone 402, six word pairs (EN + RU), word and reflection sliders per theme, reflection inks, the menu hero's rule; values remembered per browser, "Reset to site" = the numbers below.
- **Built:** `components.css` → Section Link Word: `:root` `--link-word-opacity: 0.35`, `--link-reflection-light: 0.22`, `--link-reflection-dark: 0.15` (+ `--link-reflection-light-ink` Avocado / `-dark-ink` Lime for live text). Words at the word value; head reflections light by day, dark by night and always on the bar menu (dark by day); the menu page's foot words (`--reflection` borrowed) excluded, as in `reveal.css`. Live text (About, Visit) reads the same variables by ground. **The 40 home/menu reflection SVGs recoloured** (sed on the one ink hex per file): 18 Lime → Avocado #869438 (home `dayMode`, `kitchen-day`, RU twins), 22 Paper → Lime #C1DB34 (home `nightMode`, `kitchen-night`, `bar`, RU twins); the filter stand-in and the `?refl` flag are gone. Deleted earlier in the day: night 0.3 / phones 1, day 0.5 / below-992 1, About's and Visit's per-ground SVG rules.
- **Verified:** see below. **Open:** a real phone (the night 15% on scaled hairlines is what the Sep rule protected); further tuning — the lab's numbers go into the three variables.

## Footer «Поддать жару!» on phones — on and off, not once — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author, from a phone test on sweetpepper.bar):** the item "changes just once, should be back & forth" — Figma NavItemsFooter → delay-mobile has an "After delay" arrow each way.
- **Built:** `page-loader.js` (the IntersectionObserver no longer disconnects: while the item is in view it toggles `.is-heated` — first 0.8 s after it shows, then every 1.6 s, i.e. a 0.8 s change and a 0.8 s rest; the timer stops when it scrolls away and starts again on return), `footer.css` (the 0.8 s Gentle transitions moved from `.is-heated` to a new `.is-heat-loop` set on the button, so the cooling is as slow as the heating). Reduced motion: heats once and stays, as before. Desktop with hover: unchanged.
- **Verified:** built bundle on a private copy of the home page at 375 px — Peppercorn → Chili → back, twice in 7 s.
- **Open:** a real phone; the rest length (0.8 s guessed from the old delay — Figma's delay value could not be read: the Figma connector needs re-authorising).
