## Event cards — the phone band regrouped; a frosted band behind `?band=frost` — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** fine with the desktop cards, "concerned about the spacing and overall design on mobile; they feel a bit unbalanced". Measured on the 402px card: inside the 82px band the ink stood 22px under the band's edge, 18 between date and title, 19 over the card's foot — three equal steps, so the two lines never grouped; the title shows 17 characters (spaces counted, as every count below); the band hides 27% of the photo and the photo reads through it. Eight phone options and two desktop ones went on contact sheets, mocked on the live page (the band regrouped · frosted · the caption under the photo · a square photo · a wider card · the caption as a Golos sentence · the poster alone; desktop: frosted · a two-line title with the link in the date's place).
- **The author's call:** the regrouped band is the phone default; the frosted band is a test option on phones and desktop; the desktop card and its hover stay; no square photo — the posts come from social media at 4:5.
- **Built:** `src/css/events.css`, `template-parts/home/events.php`. No JS.
  - **Phones (default):** the band hugs its copy — padding 12 / 16 (was 16), 2px between the date row and the title (was 8): **70px, was 82**, the photo gains 12px. Ink 17 / 12 / 16 with 16 at the sides.
  - **Tablet band, 768–991 (the author's iPad screenshot, same day):** the 3 × 2 grid stays, but its cards are 229–285px wide — the phone card's 240 — under the desktop body: at 768 the band was 98px on a 286px card, **a third of it** (22% on desktop), ink 30 / 18 / 27 with 24 at the sides, 14 characters of title, and neither the link line nor a source mark, since touch has no hover. The card's rules moved from the `≤ 767` block into a `≤ 991` one (*the touch card* — widened, not copied): band 70px (24–20% of the card), ink 17 / 12 / 16, sides 16, 16–20 characters, the VK / Instagram mark on the date row, pill inset 16, the phone card's colours and pinned keyline; the hover copy stack is off in the band (the card's shadow, stroke and photo lean still answer a mouse). The rail, the fixed 240 card and the blurred tile stay `≤ 767`. From 992 the desktop card is untouched. A desktop-body variant at 16px padding (band 82, the hover stack kept) was mocked beside it and dropped: it brings back the 22 / 18 / 19 steps and gives touch nothing.
  - **Touch screens wider than 991 (author, same day: "I would use the phone body for iPads"):** an iPad on its side is 1024–1366 wide and gets the desktop grid; the desktop card there kept its link line and its source behind a hover that never comes. The touch-card block's query is now `(max-width: 991px), (any-hover: none)` — the exact complement of the build's hover guard (`vite.config.js` wraps every `:hover` in `any-hover: hover`), so the hover stack and the touch card never meet. Cards 293–357px wide: band 70px (19–16% of the card), 20–26 characters, the mark on the date row. An iPad with a trackpad attached hovers, and keeps the desktop card; so does every mouse from 992 up.
  - **`?band=frost` (trial, every width, both languages):** the section takes `home-events--frost`; the band gets `backdrop-filter: blur(12px) saturate(1.2)` (with the `-webkit-` twin) and the card's inner radius on its own bottom corners (older WebKit lets a backdrop filter escape a rounded, clipping parent). Anything else in `band` does nothing. It stacks with `?title-lines=2`.
- **Checked** in headless Chrome on this branch's private server, RU and EN: the phone band 69.5px at 402 and 360 with the gaps above; the desktop band still 98 and the hover stack as before, plain and frosted, day and night; the tablet band at 768, 834 and 991 (70px, the mark showing) and 992 back on the desktop card; with Chrome's touch emulation (`any-hover: none`) at 1024, 1180 and 1366 the phone body, with a mouse at the same widths the desktop card.
- **Not checked:** Safari and any real phone — the blur's cost while the rail scrolls, and the band's corners under WebKit. A real iPad — upright, on its side, and with a trackpad attached: the no-hover rule was seen only under Chrome's touch emulation.
- **Open:** frost or plain (`testing.md`); the title is still 17 characters on phones — the roomier options stay on the sheets (`Claude outputs/event-card-ideas/`).
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → Mobile — Events / social entrance (the band's numbers; *Frosted band — on trial*; *Tablets take the touch card*), Tablet band (a pointer), Image caption label → Geometry (the pill's 16 on the touch card), *Hover only where a pointer can hover* (the complement query), Image ratios → 4:5 (not cropped); `testing.md` → *Event cards — the frosted band*, and a note on the open two-line question; `design.md` §9 (the phone card frame still draws 16 / 8 / 82; no tablet frame); the `events.css` and `event-card.php` rows of the file tables below. Sheets and scripts: `Claude outputs/event-card-ideas/` (phone, desktop, tablet, landscape iPad).

## Language switch — one thumb: the fill travels, and the move carries over the page load — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** "better ideas for the lang-switch on mobile and desktop — my main concern is the states and switch change animation". Measured on the 25 Sep switch first: the pressed state eased in over 0.8s and a tap lasts about 100ms (100ms into a press the label and keyline had not moved, the inset stood at 0.8% opacity); nothing animated the change — tap, reload, the fill had jumped; the drawer's link was 45 × 32; the tappable code was Mushroom on Parchment, 2.40:1 (hover Paprika, 2.51:1).
- **Process:** five ideas on an interactive board, each with its states frozen and a simulated page load (`Claude outputs/lang-switch-ideas/lang-switch-ideas.html` — git-ignored; it embeds the bundle and the fonts), and the same five injected into the live local page on two contact sheets (`sheet-desktop.png`, `sheet-phone.png`): **A one thumb** · B outline → fill letters, no block · C the 25 Sep frames kept, a fill that floods from the press point · D one chip naming the other language (breaks "both codes always visible"; small enough for the phone header) · E a little heat slider (argued against). The author took **A, as the default**, and kept the drawer's switch **where it sits** — the card row «Язык» mocked for A, D and E was turned down: less risk of an accidental tap, hierarchy, proximity.
- **Built:** `inc/lang.php` (the markup and the head script), `src/css/header.css` (the component — *Language switch — one thumb*; the 25 Sep states block and the About / Visit overrides are gone), `src/css/mobile-drawer.css` (the green pair and the 44px face). No module JS.
  - **Markup** — `sweet_pepper_lang_switch()` prints `data-at` (the half the thumb is on) and a decorative `.lang-switch__thumb` holding `.lang-switch__ghost`, a second copy of both codes in the label colour (`aria-hidden`). The options are unchanged: the current code a `<span aria-current>`, the other an `<a hreflang>`.
  - **The thumb** — absolute, half the pill, moved by `translateX` alone (the halves are equal: 116px in the header, was 106). The ghost is counter-translated by the same amount on the same curve, so the copy stays still against the page and each letter turns where the fill's edge crosses it.
  - **States** — rest: the other code bare, Ash by day (6.63:1), Mushroom on dark · hover: a 1px outline where the fill will land (Paprika · Cream · Avocado · Lime) and the label at full ink, 0.8s flat · pressed: outline + inset over the label, the label at full ink too, 100ms · focus: the 2px outline as before.
  - **Smaller changes that came with it** — the pill no longer clips (`overflow: hidden` is gone; the outline and the spring's overshoot stay inside its 3px padding); the browser's own grey tap flash is off on the switch (`-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent`), so the pressed state is what a phone shows; the hover outline leaves the moment the thumb sets off; in forced-colours mode the thumb keeps a 2px `Highlight` edge, since the fill is dropped there; **at night** the pill's padding went 4 → 3, so the switch — and the What's new chip, which stretches to it — is 38.4 tall in both themes (40.4 at night before); the drawer's hover outline is new (the 25 Sep drawer had no hover) and shows only where a pointer can hover (the build's hover guard).
  - **Differs from the board the author chose from:** the hover outline eases over the house 0.8s flat curve (brief → Motion language → colour-only transitions); the board ran it in 300ms.
  - **The switch** — the head script's click listener (already there for `sp-lang`) adds `lang-leaving` to `<html>` and stores the time (`sessionStorage` `sp-lang-go`); the thumb runs 500ms on `--ease-gentle`. On the next page the same script, before paint, finds the time, and if under 500ms have passed sets `--ls-el` and `lang-arriving` — the same keyframes with a negative delay — corrects the count on the first frame, and clears both 900ms later. The nudge's «Переключить» starts the move too. Not for a ⌘ / ctrl / shift / alt click or a cancelled one; `pageshow` with `persisted` (Back from the page cache) and a 20s safety put the thumb back where the markup says.
  - **Drawer** — the same component in Avocado / Lime, faces 7 × 16 so the pill is 132 × 44.4; the link's hit area reaches the pill's edge.
- **Checked** on a private server running this branch (headless Chrome, real navigations, the thumb sampled every frame): `/` → `/en/` with a 90ms press — the leaving page ran 4.0 → 49.3px in 155ms, the arriving page picked up at 57.8px 254ms after the tap and followed the same curve (60.7 peak, 58.8 home; an uninterrupted run reads 58.2 at 240ms); the ghost stayed at 4.0 throughout. A slower load (530ms) finished on the leaving page and arrived at rest. Back restored the first page with the thumb at home; a ⌘-click left it alone; reduced motion — no animation. States shot in the header (day, night, About, `/en/`) and the drawer (day, night, About's dark one) — `Claude outputs/lang-switch-ideas/built-desktop-states.png`, `built-drawer.png`; the chip beside the switch is 38.4 with it. **Again at sweet-pepper-bar.local after the sync into the main checkout:** the arriving page picked the thumb up at 47.2px, 164ms after the tap, and ran on to 60.3 → home.
- **Seen while sampling, not caused by this:** the arriving page's first one or two frames draw the pill 12px wider (127.7 against 115.6) — Molot swapping in over the fallback face, as every Molot line on the site does on load (`font-display: swap`, no preload).
- **Not checked:** an iPhone or any real touch; Safari; the test site's real network; widths other than 1440 and 402; the keyboard (Enter on the link); the nudge's «Переключить» starting the move, the 20s safety and forced colours — written, not exercised. Figma still draws the two keys (`design.md` §9).
- **Offered with the board, not built:** the page itself turning instead of blinking (a cross-document View Transition — Chrome, Edge, Safari; untested against the hero's page-load entrance); keeping the guest's place across the switch (the twin page opens at the top, so a switch from the drawer halfway down About lands on the hero); idea D's chip in the phone header, one tap from anywhere. The prototype CSS / JS for all five ideas, the capture scripts and the private-server router are in `Claude outputs/lang-switch-ideas/make/`.
- **Open (author):** the pressed state is 100ms — on 25 Sep a fast press read "too fast" (that one filled the key with Chili; this one is an outline and an inset); the drawer's day thumb, Parchment on Avocado, is 2.76:1.

## Phone menu navigation — a bar at the foot of the screen, the rail kept as a draft — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** considering a switch from the sticky section rail to a fixed bottom button that opens the drawer (the vacancy page's bar) or a floating one. Ten options went on one contact sheet, mocked on the live page at 402 × 720 (the rail · the vacancy bar as it is · its day twin · all + next · the drawer's sheet peeking · three floating openers · the rail moved to the bottom · Reserve in the bar, the rule-breaking one); a second sheet put the bar and the floating opener where it gets awkward (section top, cookie notice, pairing station, closer, night) and tried the drawer as a two-column bottom sheet. The author took **the sheet's edge**, kept **today's drawer** (the shorter sheet read too dense), then swapped the two halves — the opener left, the section word right — and made it the default: "I like it more than the existing option… keep the existing one as a draft for test".
- **Built:** `src/js/menu-nav-bar.js` + `src/css/menu-nav-bar.css`; `menu-jump-nav.js` (the opener that called it; from the bar it fills the section on show and a pick lands on the section's top), `menu-single-section.js` (a `menu-section:change` event), `menu-rail-nudge.js` (runs only for the draft), `main.js`. No PHP.
  - **The bar** — 64px, Parchment / Soft Peppercorn, 8px Lime top edge; kebab + the hero opener's label at the left gutter as a text link, the section's nav word flush right (Molot 28, Chili). One target. Rises on the house spring once the hero's opener is under the header; leaves when the pairing station has scrolled up. ≤ 991px; desktop keeps the edge tab.
  - **The headline** — the rail's rules are overridden by default in CSS (`html:not(.nav-rail)`), so the page is right before any script runs: in place, not sticky, the long headline, the other words hidden.
  - **The draft** — `?nav=rail` sets `html.nav-rail` (sessionStorage `spNavDraft`, so it survives the door to the other menu); no bar, the sticky rail with its entrance and nudge exactly as before. `?nav=edge` clears it.
- **Checked** in headless Chrome on the local site: default and draft on both menus, RU and EN, at 320 / 360 / 402 / 768 and 1440; opening from the bar and from the hero; a pick, and a re-pick of the current word, land on the section top (76px under the header); the bar yields at the hero and after the pairing station; with the script blocked the headline is already plain. Fit: no word is ever cut; the label drops to the kebab alone for «Без алкоголя» at 402, plus «Чай и кофе» at 360, and more at 320 (five RU bar sections, three EN food, one EN bar).
- **Not checked:** an iPhone — Safari's bottom bar and the safe-area inset under the bar; any real touch.
- **Open:** the device comparison (`testing.md`); a shorter RU bar label; the cookie notice covers the bar on a first visit; when settled, the bar moves into the markup and the loser's code goes (the rail's words, `menu-rail-nudge.js` and its CSS — or the bar).
- **Docs:** `website-brief.md` → Mobile — Menu page → *Navigation between sections* (new), with pointers at the rail paragraphs, Open → Rail discoverability, Tablet — Menu page, Sticky jump-nav → Trigger and Motion language → Idle self-demo.

## Team wall lightbox — built: the wall, closer; the switch is gone — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** "We keep the default lightboxes (A1 + P4 on cord, grow opening). Please apply them on the website." The prototype entry below is how it got here; this one is what the site does now.
- **What a guest gets:** on About → Team a click (a tap, Enter, Space) on a group photo opens the wall, closer — for everyone, no switch. From 768 up, sideways: the open print up to 960 × 640 in the middle, a waiting year's edge each side, the Olive line behind the pins. On phones a column: the open print edge to edge, the years above and below at 80%, one vertical cord through every pin. A Peppercorn 88% ground with the page visible through it, no blur. The print flies from its place on the strip, and back onto its own pin on closing.
- **Out with the prototype:** `?lightbox` and its tokens (`a2` the blur, `p3`, `nocord`, `fade`) are deleted with their CSS — the address now does nothing.
- **The prototype's four shortcuts, undone:**
  - *Markup in PHP.* `template-parts/about/team-lightbox.php` (new), rendered after the Team section beside the form's dialog: the dialog, a button per year with its pin, photo frame and year pill, the × as the Phosphor glyph the other dialogs use (`sweet_pepper_ph( 'x' )`). The labels go through `__()`: "Team photos by year" → «Командные фото по годам» (new in `languages/ru_RU.l10n.php`; mine — descriptive, for screen readers), "Close" → «Закрыть» and the photo's name (both already there).
  - *The large file by its size name, not by a guessed address.* `sweet_pepper_about_team()` passes `full` beside `src`: the `sp-hero` size — the 1440 × 960 crop where the upload is that large, else the upload itself (WordPress hands back the original when a size was never cut). Today's seven uploads are 1080 × 720, so that is what loads; a larger upload is picked up by itself.
  - *One easing solver.* `src/js/gentle-ease.js` (new) holds `gentleEase()`; `how-it-feels.js` imports it instead of carrying its own copy.
  - *Started from `main.js`,* its stylesheet imported there; `about-drift.js` is back to the strip alone.
- **Nothing loads until it is opened:** the dialog carries no `<img>` — each print has its two addresses as data, and the script makes the images on the first opening. Measured on the requests: before opening, the page asks for the seven 900 × 600 crops (the strip's own) and nothing else. Opening on a desktop adds three uploads — the open year and its two neighbours (0.5–0.8 MB each) — and each further year as the wall is rolled to it (`loading="lazy"`); the strip's crop shows under a print until its file arrives. On a phone opening adds nothing: the column shows the crops the strip already loaded.
- **Without JS** the dialog stays hidden and the strip's photos are plain photos — no button role, no hand cursor (the script adds both).
- **Checked** (headless Chrome, the worktree's theme on a private server, then the local site; RU and EN): 1440 × 900, 820 × 1180, 402 × 874, 375 × 667 — the flight frame by frame (largest step 56px, lands 960 × 640 in the middle), arrows / Home / End / wheel / a click on a neighbour / Tab / Esc / × / the ground, focus in and out, a reopening inside the closing flight, reduced motion (in place from the first frame, no roll), no JS, the Russian and English labels, the quote wheel still rolling on the shared solver, home / menu / Visit / a 404 without errors. PHP linted, div nesting counted. **Not checked:** Safari and a real phone (scroll-snap in a column, `dvh`, the flight), a screen reader, a wall photo larger than 1440 in the admin.
- **Owed:** a real-phone look — is 1.7× enough to find a face (a sideways pan at 3.3× was the alternative on the first sheet); larger originals for the wall (1080px is soft at 960 on a 2× screen — upload 1440 wide or more and the lightbox takes the 1440 crop); the Figma frames (`design.md` §9); "To be continued…" as a last slide — offered, not asked for.
- **Deploy:** the theme only — no field group, no database step. Test site and main site: the usual pull and rsync (`website-brief.md` → Test site → *Every update*); on the main site press **Delete Cache** afterwards if WP Super Cache is on (the cached pages name the old bundle, which the rsync removes).
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → *Team wall lightbox*, Interaction rule → The pepper cursor, Content editing (row 7 and the photo note); `testing.md` → Team wall lightbox; `design.md` §9.

## Team wall lightbox — a prototype behind `?lightbox` — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** Next Up 3 — the group photos on the About wall (`.about-team__drift`) should open large, "but I want to keep some elements from the drift, like the wall pin". Five directions went on one contact sheet, mocked on the live page at 1440 and 402 (A the wall, closer · B the classic dark lightbox · C the print grows on the line with no second room · D the pins as the pager, the rule-breaking one · E the print turned on its side, phones only). The author took **A**, asked for a see-through ground, and brought the phone layout: **a column, the other years above and below the open one**. A second sheet weighed the ground (dark / the team Parchment, each with and without a blur) and four columns; the author tried A1, A2, P3 and P4 in the browser and set the defaults.
- **Built — a PROTOTYPE, nothing final:** `src/js/team-lightbox.js` + `src/css/team-lightbox.css`, started from `about-drift.js` (not `main.js`, which the 404 session had open). No PHP, no markup in the template: without `?lightbox` the page has no listener and no dialog. No library.
  - **The wall, closer.** A click on a print opens the strip at lightbox scale on a Peppercorn 88% ground: each print keeps its pin (Lime / Paprika, alternating as on the strip), its 1° tilt and the Lemon year pill (24px in on desktop, 16 on phones — the caption label, untouched). The open print is straight and at full strength; the waiting years sit under a 60% Peppercorn veil with the pin at 55% — the print itself stays opaque, so the page never shows through a photo.
  - **From 768 up — sideways,** as the strip runs: the Olive line (6px) behind the prints, pins 96 × 48, the open print `min(960px, the width less a neighbour's edge each side, (height − 160) × 1.5)` — 960 × 640 at 1440 × 900 (2.7× the strip's 356), 549 × 366 at 820.
  - **Phones — a column (author).** Default **P4 on the cord**: the open print edge to edge (402px, 1.7× the strip's 240), the years above and below at 80%, one 4px vertical line through every pin. `p3` keeps every print 16px in from the edges (370px, all the same size); `nocord` takes the line away.
  - **Ground:** default **A1**, the ground alone — the strip's own prints show faintly in the gaps. `a2` blurs the page 12px behind it (`backdrop-filter`, as the cookie notice does).
  - **Opening — undecided:** `grow` (default) flies the print from its place on the strip to the middle, 800ms on Gentle, and back on closing (400ms, flat curve) — the strip is first rolled to the year the guest was looking at, so the print lands on its own pin. `fade` settles it in the middle from 94%.
  - **Moving:** the scroller is native (scroll-snap), so a swipe, a trackpad and a touch drag work by themselves; a click on a waiting year, ← → ↑ ↓, Home / End and a mouse wheel (one year per gesture) roll to it — 700ms on the house spring, snap off during the roll (Motion language → Scroll tweens). Esc, the × and a click on the ground close.
  - **Keyboard and screen readers:** the strip's prints become `role="button"` (Enter / Space, an Olive focus ring, the photo's alt as the name); the lightbox is a `role="dialog"` with two Tab stops, the open print and the ×. Opened with a pointer, focus parks on the dialog itself — no ring lit by a click (the mail chooser's fix); with the keyboard, on the open print. On closing, focus goes to the strip's print of the year last looked at — not always the one that opened it.
  - **The hand cursor (author):** the pepper cursor was built first — a print that enlarges changes, it does not navigate — and the author replaced it: "give it a pointer cursor". On the strip's prints and the waiting years; the open print keeps the arrow (a click on it does nothing).
  - **Photos:** phones show the strip's own 900 × 600 crop (already loaded); from 768 up the print loads the upload itself — 1080 × 720, its address guessed from the crop's `-900x600` name, the crop underneath until it arrives and instead of it if it fails.
- **One bug on the way:** scroll-snap follows a *transformed* snap target, so the flying print dragged the scroller 426px sideways in its first frame. The flight now transforms an inner box (`.team-lightbox__print`); the button that snaps never moves.
- **Where the prototype leaves the documented system:**
  - The hand, not the pepper cursor (`website-brief.md` → Interaction rule → The pepper cursor) — the author's call, noted there.
  - P4's open print has no 16px gutter (Grid → Mobile: 16px gutters → 370 content) — it is the point of P4.
  - The cord is vertical; the brief's "a clothesline is horizontal by nature" (Mobile — About page → Team) was said of the page's one rail, and the strip itself is unchanged.
  - Always dark, over a Parchment section — About is a fixed composition, and a Parchment veil over the waiting photos would be the wash the brief retired (Day / night pairings → Revision, Jul 2026).
- **Shortcuts to undo in the real build:** the markup and the two labels («Командные фото по годам», «Закрыть» / "Team photos by year", "Close") are made in the script, not in PHP and the `.po`; the large file is guessed from a file name instead of a registered image size; `gentleEase()` is a second copy of `how-it-feels.js`'s; the start is in `about-drift.js`.
- **Checked** (headless Chrome, the worktree's theme on a private server, then the local site, RU and EN): without the flag — no dialog, no role, no cursor, a click does nothing; 1440 × 900, 820 × 1180, 402 × 874, 375 × 667; the flight sampled frame by frame (starts on the strip's print, largest step 57px, lands 960 × 640 in the middle); arrows, Home / End, wheel, a click on a neighbour, Tab, Esc, ×, the ground; focus in and out, no ring after a pointer; **reduced motion** — no flight, no roll, the print is in place from the first frame; **a finger swipe on the strip scrolls it and opens nothing**, a tap opens, a swipe inside the column moves one year (touch emulation); no console errors. **Not checked:** Safari and a real phone (the snap, the blur, `dvh`), a screen reader, the test site (not deployed).
- **Owed:** the author's verdict on the opening, and whether `a2` / `p3` / `nocord` / `fade` can go → the real build (PHP markup and labels, a larger image size — and larger originals if they exist: 1080px is soft at 960 on a 2× screen — `main.js`, the switch deleted); a Figma frame (none exists); "To be continued…" as the last slide was offered, not asked for.
- **Not committed.** Three files in the main checkout (two new, `about-drift.js` + 2 lines); the bundle there was built from the main checkout's sources, so it holds the 404 session's styles too.
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → *Team wall lightbox — on trial*, Interaction rule → The pepper cursor; `testing.md` → Team wall lightbox; `design.md` §9.

## 404 page — three layouts on trial behind `?nf=` — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** Next Up 1 — the theme had no `404.php`. The author's Figma draft ("Lost in the Sauce-404" 2803:78133; the digits component `lettersSize` 2807:77957) was read against `design.md` and the brief, five other directions went on one contact sheet (A the bar answers on a ticket · B the number sinking into the footer · C "there's no table 404", Reserve as the action · D the nav list with nothing filled · E two doors, the rule-breaking one), and the author picked **0** (the draft), **A** and **B** to prototype, with my suggested copy first — "I'll refine it after seeing it in the browser".
- **Built — a PROTOTYPE, nothing decided:** `404.php` (the theme's first), `inc/not-found.php`, `data/not-found.php`, `template-parts/not-found/digits.php` + `ticket.php`, `src/css/not-found.css`; `functions.php` requires the inc, `main.js` imports the styles. One template; the layout is picked on the URL of any address that doesn't exist — nothing or `?nf=0` · `?nf=a` · `?nf=b` · `?nf=bt`.
  - **0 — the draft as drawn.** 28 · the digits' room (353) · 26 · the notice line · 26 · headline (Molot 36) · 24 · copy (560 wide) · 40 · buttons · 93, all centred. The digits are live Molot at 238px, a 2px outline, and run the component's four states as one 6 s CSS loop: rest Chili → the first 4 Lemon → the 0 Lime → the last 4 Paprika; the popped digit is × 1.176 and its neighbours step 12.5px aside (the Figma states' geometry, sampled per state in the browser: 140 × 214 → 165 × 252). `scale` / `translate` only, Bouncy for the digits, the flat curve for the colour. Sizes are in em, so a phone scales the set whole (`min(238px, 47vw)`). **The notice line sits under the number** — it opened the page at first; the author, in the browser: above the number it "argued with the top nav", and the Figma frame was changed the same way.
  - **a — the bar answers.** Copy left (6 columns), **the dish picker's own ticket** right: the same wrap, edges, card and type classes (`dish-picker.css`), 404px wide, Cream by night on desktop and Parchment below 992 as the component already is, printing on arrival (`data-reveal="mask-down"`, the house spring). It prints the address the guest asked for — the path only (a query can carry anything), language prefix dropped, percent-decoded so Cyrillic reads, control characters stripped, 60 characters at most, escaped on output. Copy and ticket are centred in the screen under the header (`100svh` less the header); the footer starts at the fold.
  - **b — the number in the footer.** The draft's copy left in a 400px column (the headline may run to 6 columns — «В СТОП-ЛИСТЕ.» broke at the hyphen at 400), the giant outlined 404 right, 0.16em of it under the footer's edge. The theater is 240px shorter than the screen, so the footer — hours, phone, links — is in the first screen. The footer's bites are real holes on this layout (a mask on `.site-footer`, the bites' paint switched off), so the digits show through them. **A Chili level rises in the digits and drains, 9 s** — my suggestion from the feedback, not the author's component; no verdict yet.
  - **bt — b with the ticket.** The author's note read "in option B, we should use the existing ticket component and refine the alignment (vertically centered on the page)"; the ticket was option A on the sheet, so I applied it to A and kept `bt` as the other reading. Crowded — the ticket covers the 0.
- **Always dark — the prototype's assumption, not yet a decision:** `inc/daypart-head.php` sets night on a 404 before paint and fixes the browser chrome's Peppercorn; the footer's bites take Peppercorn (`not-found.css`). Only night is drawn in Figma; a day version would need its own colours (the Lemon headline and the Lime line fail on Paper).
- **Words:** `data/not-found.php`, EN and RU side by side. Layouts 0 and b — the author's Figma lines in English, my Russian twins («Кухня сообщает · Столик 404», «Эта страница в стоп-листе.», «На главную» / «Смотреть меню»). Layout a and the ticket — mine in both languages ("That one's off the menu." / «Такого в меню нет.»; ticket: Order 404 / «Заказ 404», "The bar says" / «Бар отвечает», the pot-roast line, a link to Hot dishes). The author's pass is owed on all of it.
- **The switch was `?pick=` for its first hour and clashed with the page loader**, which reads `?pick=a…f` on every page and keeps it for the tab: a 404 opened with `?pick=a` or `?pick=b` also pinned the loader's look a or b. Renamed `?nf=` once found (the docs check, the same day). A tab that opened the old links: `?pick=auto` lets the loader go, or close the tab.
- **Where the prototype leaves the documented system — built as drawn, to settle with the layout:**
  - The notice line is Molot 16 (`Heading/Captions`) in Lime with an 8px Chili dot. Eyebrows are H3 18 and Cream by night, and the author ruled in Sep that `Heading/Captions` is not sanctioned (the phone section header entry below; `design.md` §3.3).
  - The headline is Lemon; every other title is Chili on its first line and Paprika after (Title lines, 25 Sep). In layout 0 it is 36px at every width, not `--text-h1`.
  - Layout 0 centres its short copy (`website-brief.md` → Grid: short copy is never centred).
  - In 0 and b the filled button is Home; the brief calls the menu the highest-intent destination (layout a puts the menu first).
- **Checked** (headless Chrome, RU and EN): 402 × 874, 834 × 1112, 1024 × 768, 1280 × 900, 1440 × 900, 1920 × 1080 — no sideways scroll, the answer is a real 404, the night theme is on before paint, `/`, `/en/` and `/menu/food/` still load; the ticket's print sampled frame by frame; a `<script>` path prints escaped, a 90-character path is cut, a Cyrillic path reads, invalid UTF-8 prints `/`. **Not checked:** Safari and a real phone; reduced motion (the animations are gated in CSS, not looked at); keyboard order; `bt` on a phone.
- **Owed:** the author's verdict → delete the losing layouts and the switch; the copy pass; a phone frame in Figma (none exists); the page title in the site's voice (it is WordPress's "Page not found" / «Страница не найдена»); the share tags (`inc/seo.php` prints the missing address as `og:url`); the "always dark" ruling.
- **Not committed.** The files are in the 404 worktree and copied into the main checkout for the local site; the bundle there was rebuilt by a parallel session afterwards (it holds these styles).
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → What themes and what doesn't (the 404 row), The bartender's ticket (Used in), *404 page — on trial*; `design.md` §3.3; `testing.md` → 404 page.

## Page loader — the lines move into admin: «Пасхалка» — 1 October 2026

- **Why (author):** the team should change the loader's lines themselves — a seasonal greeting, an idea of their own. Until now a line was a code edit in two lists (`data/page-loader.php`).
- **Built:** one record, **«Пасхалка»** ("the Easter egg" — the author's name for it, easier for the team; «Экран загрузки» for its first hour), in the admin sidebar (`page_loader` post type, `inc/cpt.php`) — the «Гастробот» pattern: one post, no second can be added, revisions and the edit lock. A post rather than the Bar Settings tab once considered: the author asked for a page/post, and an options page has no revisions. Field group `group_sp_loader` (generated, `tools/page-field-groups.py`): a table, a row per line — **Строка · Строка (EN) · Продолжение · Скрыть**, drag to reorder.
- **Продолжение** replaces the code's bracketed chain: the row always plays right after the one above («Солим…» → «Пересолили…» → «Придётся…» is three rows, the last two ticked). **Скрыть** takes a row off the site and keeps it in the list — a seasonal line waits for its season; hidden on the row a chain starts with, it hides the whole chain (a continuation says nothing alone).
- **Length is stopped at the form:** 34 characters in Russian, 40 in English (`SWEET_PEPPER_LOADER_LINE_MAX`, `inc/page-loader.php`) — the longest lines that hold one row today; a longer one gets a message with the way out (shorten, or split with «Продолжение»). Counted in characters, so it is a guard, not a measurement: `page-loader-copy.md` has the pixels.
- **Languages:** the RU line is required; an empty EN borrows the Russian (the site's rule, `sweet_pepper_pick()`), which also keeps both languages in the same positions — the language switch carries a line by its position.
- **Not in admin, on purpose:** «Разогреваем!», «Подано!», the name, the screen-reader status — the layout is fitted to them. `data/page-loader.php` keeps them, and its pool is now the seed and the fallback (no record, or every row hidden → the typed pool renders; a loader with no lines never ships).
- **Cache:** the lines print into every page, and a page cache purges only the saved post's own address — saving the record clears all of WP Super Cache (`acf/save_post`, as Bar Settings does).
- **Seeded locally:** `tools/page-seed.php loader` → record 3222, 16 rows. Checked through the worktree's theme: `/`, `/en/`, `/about/`, `/en/menu/bar/` print the same `data-lines` / `data-chains` as before, byte for byte; chain head hidden (13 entries, no chain), chain middle hidden (a chain of two), a first row ticked «Продолжение» (ignored), a row with no English (the Russian shows on `/en/`), every row hidden (typed pool); 35 RU / 41 EN characters are refused, 34 / 40 pass; the form renders 16 filled rows. **Not seen by me:** the admin screen itself (it needs the author's login). **Owed:** the test site and the main site — SCF sync + the seeder (`website-brief.md` → Test site → the 1 Oct steps).
- **Asked, not built:** dates on a line (show from / until) — the page is cached, so it would have to be filtered in the browser, and positions would differ between pages; «Скрыть» by hand does it for now. A line that opens every run (a greeting most guests would actually see — in a random pool of 14 where a wait shows one or two, a given line comes up about one wait in ten).
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → Page loader (words), Platform (the editing list), Test site (steps); `page-loader-copy.md`; `testing.md` → Page loader.

## Page loader — the footer replay becomes a toy; one Russian line shortened — 1 October 2026

- **Why:** a tester opened the loader from the footer, called it "like an Easter egg" and tried to drag the knob. The author: make that replay interactive — drag, a reset that shows other words, a clear way back.
- **Built, on by default** (`page-loader.js` → ON TRIAL; `page-loader.css`; `footer.css`): the replay stays on «Подано!»; the knob drags (pointer events on the slider; tap the track; ← → Home End), each new daypart brings the next line, the end serves «Подано!» and dragging back takes it off; the shaker (the dish picker's own markup, so its hover, pressed, night glow and spin) starts the show over; × or Esc goes back; the knob nudges left and back every 4 s until the slider is touched; the controls come in at the end of the show or at the first touch. On a touch screen or in a phone-wide window (≤ 767) the footer's item takes its heat look 0.8 s after it shows — the width was added so the state can be seen from a desktop's responsive mode, where a mouse is still present (the author could not find it there). Real waits between pages are untouched.
- **Three layouts, the author's Figma frames for two of them:** 3 (default) — wide screens: the shaker centred above the slider, «Вернуться на сайт» under the name, × in the top corner (2792:75651); upright tablets: a row above the words, shaker left, × right; phones: the same row, × in the top corner. `?egg=1` — the row on every screen. `?egg=2` — «Назад» and «Встряхнуть!» under the name on wide screens (2792:75652). `?egg=0` — the plain replay.
- **Shaker size:** a 64px disc everywhere in layout 3 — up from the picker's 40 on phones (author), down from the frame's 84 on desktops (agreed with the author: 84 is the name's cap height and took the eye from the slider).
- **As drawn, against the system:** in layout 3 on wide screens the disc is Lemon by night (the picker's night disc is Cream with a Lemon keyline) and × is Parchment. The author to confirm.
- **«Пересчитываем горошины в перечнице» → «Считаем горошины в перечнице»** (`data/page-loader.php`): the one line in two rows on the author's iPhone. The side padding of 32px made every phone column 64px narrower than the screen; measured again — from 390 up every line holds one row in both languages; at 375 three RU lines and one EN still break (`page-loader-copy.md` → the table, with shorter candidates).
- Checked by script in headless Chrome (mouse and touch): the default on 1440 × 900, 393 × 852, 820 × 1180; `?egg=0`, 1, 2; hover and pressed states; the reworded line at 393. **Owed:** real devices; screen-reader semantics for the slider and focus moved into the loader if it stays; the controls and their RU labels (mine: «Вернуться на сайт», «Назад», «Встряхнуть!», «Выбирай огонёк!», «Ещё раз») move from the script into the part and `ru_RU.l10n.php` once a layout wins.

## Page loader — prototypes for tablet and desktop: six looks behind `?pick=` — 1 October 2026

The author, after the phone default: a prototype for the desktop with BAR; on tablets keep SWEET PEPPER, a word at a time, and try the poster there.
- **One switch for every screen.** The name's look is two attributes, `data-name` (short · poster · row) and `data-fill` (together · words), set by the part's inline script from the screen's default or `?pick=a…f` (`?pick=auto` lets go; it re-reads on a turned phone or a resized window). CSS is written once per look, not once per screen. Without the script the loader is short · together.
- **Defaults:** phones held upright **d** (poster, a word at a time) · tablets 600–991 **b** (SWEET PEPPER, a word at a time — the author's) · desktops **a** (unchanged).
- **New on wide screens:** the poster as a centred column (520px; it narrows to fit a short window — 424 at 1366 × 620, never under 240; its words stack), and SWEET PEPPER BAR in one 1100px row (e, f) — the first desktop frame's size.
- **The phone layout ends at 599** (was 767): an iPad mini upright showed BAR 266px tall. From 600 a screen takes the wide layout. The earlier entry's `?pick=a|b|c|d` and `data-word` are replaced by this.
- Checked: every default at 320, 402, 599, 600, 744, 768, 991, 992, 1440 and 667 × 375; picks b–f at 1440; d at 834 × 1194, 1024 × 768, 1366 × 620, 667 × 375; a, b, c, e, f on the phone (e and f fall back to the poster there). Headless Chrome only. **Owed:** the author's verdicts for phone, tablet and desktop → delete the losing looks and the switch.
- **After the commit — the first day's results and the tablet default (1 Oct):** phones d (testers, and the owner at once); desktop a (three words read as a longer wait); tablets a or two fitted lines, not the poster (iPad mini, 11-inch Air; the 13-inch Pro pending). The "sweet pepper version in tablets" the author liked was the *earlier* build on an upright iPad mini — the phone layout's two fitted lines on the bottom edge — not the centred row I had made the tablet default. **Built:** the stacked bottom-edge layout now covers every upright screen up to 1100 wide (`page-loader.css`; the column is the screen less its side padding, the line gap and the bleed scale with it), default b from 600; on its side every screen is a. Checked at 402, 600, 744, 820, 834, 1024, 1032, 1100 / 1101 upright and 667 × 375, 1133 × 744, 1180 × 820, 1366 × 1024, 1440 on their sides; picks a, c, d, e on the upright Air. The log and the test links: `testing.md` → *Page loader*.

## Page loader on phones — the poster with BAR, a word at a time — 1 October 2026

- **Films for testers, not screen recordings:** four options of the phone loader as MP4 files (`Claude outputs/page-loader-videos/`, 804 × 1748, 60 fps, 9.2 s, RU and EN; a combined RU film with A / B / C / D cards) — A: SWEET / PEPPER under the knob · B: the same, a word at a time · C: the poster with BAR under the knob · D: the poster, a word at a time. Captured from the real local page with a stepped clock in headless Chrome and encoded to H.264 there (no ffmpeg on this Mac); checked frame by frame with Apple's decoder. The scripts are in `…/page-loader-videos/make/`.
- **3 of 3 testers picked D**, so D is the phone's default while the author keeps testing on the real site. Built: a third SVG, BAR (179.1 × 70), shown only on phones held upright; each line takes `1 / --words` of the track (`page-loader.css` → the phone block). **`?pick=a|b|c`** shows another option for the browser tab, `?pick=d` lets go — it replaces `?fill=`.
- **What D changes:** on phones the heat's edge is no longer under the knob; BAR starts only past two thirds of the track, so on a 1–3 s wait it stays an outline until the run to «Подано!». Wide screens are untouched (SWEET PEPPER, centred, under the knob).
- **A slip caught before it shipped:** the switch first wrote `data-lines`, the attribute that already holds the loader's line pool — picks A and B broke the loader. It writes `data-word="short"` now.
- Checked at 402 (20 / 50 / 80% and served: 40% of SWEET · half of PEPPER · 40% of BAR · all), 320, 667 × 375, 744 × 1133, 1440; picks a, b, c and back to d. **Owed:** the author's verdict → delete the losing options and the switch; an iPad mini upright shows BAR 266px tall (end the phone layout at 599 if that is too much).

## Page loader — one group: the words, the slider, SWEET PEPPER — 1 October 2026

The author's question: on a desktop the loader looked better than on a phone — the viewport ratio, the slider's height?
- **What was wrong on the phone:** not the slider. The one-line word was 26px tall, 3% of the screen (9.5% on a desktop), the slider ran edge to edge like a rule across the middle, and the two had different left edges (16 and 32px).
- **Chosen from contact sheets** (variants injected into the live local page at 402 × 874): round 1 — a taller slider alone, the word in two and three lines, a poster, the word turned up the left edge (the rule-breaker); round 2, after the author dropped BAR and asked for the slider or the copy nearer the words — **option F: the words, then the slider lying on the name, SWEET / PEPPER in two fitted lines**, standing on the bottom edge.
- **Desktop followed.** The author liked one name everywhere but saw the bigger word taking attention from the lines; of seven mock-ups two were built — the same group on the bottom edge and in the middle — and **the middle one stays** (the author's check on the local site with one guest).
- **Built** (`src/css/page-loader.css`, `template-parts/components/page-loader.php`, `data/page-loader.php`): one group on every screen; the name is SWEET PEPPER in both languages (the `word` copy key is gone), one SVG per word — a row on wide screens (`min(825px, 100%)`, letters 85px), two fitted lines on phones held upright; track 12 / knob 36; the knob's centre runs the whole track, so the heat's edge is under it (within 0.1px at 320, 402, 667 × 375, 768, 834, 1024, 1440, 1920, day and night, RU and EN, 55%, 90% and served); side padding never under 32px. The words sit above the slider: a wrapped line no longer moves it. No script change.
- **On trial:** `?fill=words` (the phone's name a word at a time; `?fill=together` lets go). **Owed:** the author's check on a real phone → delete the losing fill and the switch in the part's inline script; WebKit's bottom bar over the group is unchecked (headless Chrome only). Figma: redraw the two frames, add a phone frame (`design.md` §9).
- Docs: `website-brief.md` → Motion language → Page loader; `page-loader-copy.md` (the name row); `design.md` §9.

## Visit — the landmark badges' hover, pressed and active states — 30 September 2026

- **Figma had them, the site didn't (author):** locationBadge 945:32243 draws Default · hover (Paper, Paprika keyline, hint line shown) · pressed (Cream, Olive, inset) · active (Cream). The site had rest and active only, and only `background-color` in its transition.
- **Built (`visit.css`):** hover Paper + Paprika; pressed Cream + inset, 100ms; active Cream with the default cursor and no hover/press (location-map.js ignores a click on it); border and shadow now ease with the ground. Text colours never change — no mid-fade dip.
- **The hint line:** shown on hover as drawn, it grew a badge 41.4 → 59.6px and jumped the three below it 18px (measured at 1280; at the tablet grid a whole row grew). Three ways tried on the page (`?badgehint=` flag, now removed): colour only, Figma's reveal, a slide — **the author picked the slide**: `max-height` + opacity on the flat curve, 800ms, ≥ 768 (sampled: 41.4 → 46.6 → 56.0 → 59.6px at 0 / 200 / 400 / 800ms, no overshoot); off under reduced motion. The phone rule hiding an active hint went (the base already hides it below 768).
- **Sweet Pepper Bar active by default (`location-map.js` → `restRoutes`):** switching maps off cleared every badge, and allowing them loaded the door's map with no badge marked. Now the door is active on load, after maps off, and after maps on; a chosen route falls back to the door when maps go off. Checked in the browser.
- **A sync slip, fixed:** the first prototype sync copied the new CSS bundle but not the rebuilt JS one; the local site's scripts were missing for about a minute. New bundles now go across before the manifest.

## Night secondary button — a Lime tint instead of the Cream swap — 30 September 2026

- **The fault (author: "the same easing problem as the primary"):** the night secondary's hover faded the ground to Cream while the label faded Lime → Peppercorn on the same 0.8s curve; sampled every 20ms (and in Chrome), label and ground met in the same olive at ~260ms — **1.13:1 on Peppercorn, 1.03:1 on Soft Peppercorn**, under 3:1 from 220 to 320ms, both ways. The Lime cookie band's «Got it» swapped its two colours exactly: **1.00:1** at ~280ms. Day secondary fine (4.2 → 16.5, only rises).
- **Built (author's pick of three rounds):** `buttons.css` — one dark-ground rule for night, `.mobile-drawer--dark`, `.about-section--dark`, `.page-vacancy`, `.cookie-notice--dark` and `.btn-secondary--dark`: hover **25% Lime ground**, pressed **15% Lime + inset**, label always Lime (11.8 / 6.4 / 8.5:1). The five copies in `mobile-drawer.css`, `about.css` (×2), `vacancy.css` and `cookie-notice.css` are gone. Every secondary's press is 100ms, as the primary's. `cookie-notice.css` — the band's button: Peppercorn 15% hover, 25% + inset pressed (8.8 / 7.0:1).
- **Rounds (kept: `Claude outputs/secondary-night-hover/`, README inside):** (1) Cream label · Lime tint · label jumps at the crossover · 200ms swap; (2) tint with the dark cards' glow, a button-sized glow, glow only; (3) tint vs glow-only beside the primary — the tint answers by colour alone, as the primary does. The author swapped the tint's states (25% hover, 15% pressed) so the press reads deeper.
- **Checked in the browser:** a real mouse hover on a night «Food menu» gives `rgba(193, 219, 52, 0.25)` with a Lime label; rest states on About and Visit unchanged (Lime on the dark grounds, Peppercorn on the band). Bundle `main-YKD5J77S.css`.
- **Not changed, noted:** `hero.css` still carries unscoped `.btn-primary, .btn-secondary` rules (`border: none`, padding 12/24, `color: var(--text)`) that lose only because `buttons.css` loads after it.

## First visit: the blank screen, Phosphor's web font retired, page-cache steps — 30 September 2026

- **The question (author):** the page loader shows between pages, but a first visit is a blank white screen (iPhone, Chrome) — can a loader or a skeleton cover it? **No:** the loader lives in the page, and nothing of the page exists yet. Measured on sweetpepper.bar: the server builds every page in 0.9–2.8 s (no page cache), then the browser waits for the stylesheet and — until today — a blocking unpkg.com script that pulled six Phosphor stylesheets from jsdelivr. Skeletons suit pages filled in later by JS; this one arrives whole. Timeline and reasoning: `website-brief.md` → Hosting → *First visit, measured*.
- **Phosphor's web font retired** (the second wait): the 13 glyphs it drew are theme SVGs — `assets/icons/ph/` (from `@phosphor-icons/core` 2.1.1, MIT), `sweet_pepper_ph()` in `inc/inline-svg.php`, the unpkg `<script>` gone from `header.php`, `.ph-icon` in `components.css`. **No visual change, checked:** every icon photographed before and after on home (1280, 402), About and the food menu, plus the three × in their opened panels — box geometry identical, ink identical within 0.5px (34 of 34); the first vertical offset tried (-0.125em) put the reserve drawer's × 1.5px low, the font's own descent (-0.0625em) matches. The home hero's menu button keeps all four daypart glyphs and CSS shows one by `data-icon` (`button.php` → `icon_set`) — the `design.md` rule against JS editing an icon. Five pages load nothing from unpkg or jsdelivr now.
- **WP Super Cache:** set-up steps for the main site written into the brief (Hosting, same place); the author installs it in wp-admin. Not on the test site (it would hide changes being tested).

## About Team strip no longer drags vertically on phones — 29 September 2026

The author's iPhone (Chrome): a slightly diagonal swipe on the wall-photo strip moved it up and down. `.about-team__drift` had `overflow-x: auto; overflow-y: visible` — and beside a scrolling axis `visible` computes to `auto`; on phones (`padding-bottom: 0`) the photos' 1° tilt put 2px of corner below the box, so the strip had a 2px vertical scroll range and iOS gave it the vertical part of the swipe. Now `overflow-y: hidden` (no vertical scrolling by hand) and the phone strip gets `padding-bottom: 8px` with `margin-bottom: -8px`, so the corners aren't clipped and the cards below don't move. Measured at 375 / 402 / 1440: scroll height = client height, nothing clipped, the gap to the next block unchanged.

## English maps without asking, for the US and Canada — 29 September 2026

The author: the English page is mostly a portfolio, read by clients and employers in the US and Canada; the permission step is friction there. Advised against first (152-ФЗ binds ООО «Перец» whoever visits; the published policy §5.2 promises every visitor maps only after a choice; EU visitors would need opt-in); the author accepted that and will add the note to the privacy policy. Europe was set aside by the author.

- **`inc/geo.php`** — `sweet_pepper_maps_open()`: true on `/en/` for an IP in `data/geo-na.php`; the Russian page, Russian IPs and everyone else keep the step. `?na-test` shows the US/Canada version from anywhere, `?ru-test` the permission version; filter `sweet_pepper_maps_open`. Adds `data-maps-open` to `<html>` (the `language_attributes` filter), which `map-permission.js` → `mapAllowed()` reads as allowed. The lookup is now `sweet_pepper_ip_in( $list, $ip )` for either list; `sweet_pepper_ip_in_russia()` wraps it. The admin `sp-geo` comment also reports "US/Canadian IP" and "maps without asking".
- **Where open:** no placeholder (the map loads with the page, as a remembered permission did), no Map settings dialog or footer link, and the cookie note's map sentence reads “The maps are Google’s: loading them shares your IP address and browser information with Google.” (short: “The maps are Google’s and see your IP address.”).

## Forms send to hello@sweetpepper.bar — 29 September 2026

Both forms now deliver: the contact form (home desktop, Visit) and the About team form post to one REST route, `POST /wp-json/sweet-pepper/v1/message` (`inc/forms.php`), which sends a plain-text letter to hello@sweetpepper.bar with `wp_mail()`. No plugin (Plugin cap).

- **The letter:** Russian, subject «Сайт · <topic or сообщение>: <name>» / «Сайт · команде (Лера, Антон): <name>»; body: name, email or phone, recipients / topic, the message, then the form, the page's language and URL. **Reply-To is the guest's email** — «Ответить» in the mailbox writes back to them. Team chips arrive as names («вся команда» when none or all are picked); every letter goes to the one mailbox.
- **Browser:** `src/js/form-send.js` (shared) — the success screen shows only after the server answers ok; while sending the button is disabled (`aria-busy`, 60% opacity); a failure shows `.form-send-error` above the button with the draft's line (`forms-copy-ru-draft.md` §6, new string in `ru_RU.l10n.php`) and keeps the typed text.
- **Spam, without a nonce** (a nonce in a cached page goes stale within a day and would reject real guests): honeypot field `website`, a 3-second minimum from render to send, 10 sends per IP per hour (429). A caught bot is answered ok, nothing sent. Server re-checks name, message, email/phone (6 digits) — 400.
- **Settings in `wp-config.php`, per install, all optional:** `SWEET_PEPPER_FORMS_TO` (default hello@sweetpepper.bar); `SWEET_PEPPER_SMTP_HOST` / `_PORT` (465 → SSL, else STARTTLS) / `_USER` / `_PASS` — with them, mail leaves through that SMTP server From the login address instead of the host's sendmail (From `wordpress@sweetpepper.bar`, which may land in spam).
- **Verified** through Local's PHP with mail captured (`pre_wp_mail`): email, phone-only, team with two / no recipients, a CR/LF name (flattened to one line), honeypot, too fast, invalid (400), rate limit (10 × 200, then 429), the SMTP hook's settings. Not verified: a real letter reaching the mailbox — on Local it lands in Local's Mailpit; on the servers it needs a test send.
- **Mail route (author, 29 Sep 2026):** the hello@ mailbox is on Timeweb, forwarded to Yandex Mail. DNS checked the same day: MX `mx1/mx2.timeweb.ru`, SPF `include:_spf.timeweb.ru ~all`, DKIM selector `dkim`, DMARC `p=none`. So each install sends through **`smtp.timeweb.ru`, port 465, login hello@sweetpepper.bar** (the four `SWEET_PEPPER_SMTP_*` lines in its `wp-config.php`; the password never goes in the repo). The letter is then From hello@ and signed by Timeweb, and Yandex receives it as forwarded mail.
- **First sends landed in Yandex spam (30 Sep 2026) — headers read:** the site side is right (authenticated through `smtp.timeweb.ru`, From hello@, DKIM-signed `d=sweetpepper.bar s=dkim`, Timeweb filed it in INBOX). Yandex does not receive it by SMTP forwarding but **fetches it by POP3 (Yandex mail collector from `imap.timeweb.ru`)**, then forwards it inside Yandex — collected mail gets no SPF/DKIM verdict, so Yandex judges a new sender writing «from hello@ to hello@» with a Gmail Reply-To on content alone. Fix is on the mailbox side: «Не спам» + a Yandex filter (From hello@sweetpepper.bar → Inbox, not spam), or replace the collector with forwarding set up in Timeweb's mail panel so Yandex can verify the signature. No code change.
- **Replies from Yandex as hello@ (30 Sep 2026, Gmail «Показать оригинал»):** SPF, DKIM (`d=sweetpepper.bar`) and DMARC all PASS — Yandex sends the collected address through Timeweb's SMTP (IP 92.53.116.151), so the collector setup needs no DNS change. The From name is the manager's own («Примышев Юрий <hello@…>»), set in Yandex.
- **Topic chips on desktop too (author, 29 Sep 2026):** the Visit form's four chips were phones only (`visit.css` hid them above 767px). Now at every width: the dark-form chip colours moved out of the phone block, and above 767px the row wraps (`contacts.css`) — a hidden-scrollbar strip can't be scrolled with a mouse. Measured at 1280 (EN): the form row is 463px, the chips need 501 with gaps, so «Any questions» takes a second line; RU is shorter. The home form still has no topics.
- **Consent (29 Sep 2026, author's choices):** a separate page, and the letter as the record. Both forms end with an unticked, required checkbox «Даю [согласие] на обработку персональных данных» (`sweet_pepper_consent_field()` in `inc/forms.php`); «согласие» opens `/consent/` in a new tab so the typed message stays. The server refuses a message without it (400), and each letter's last line is the record: «Согласие на обработку персональных данных: дано 30.09.2026 04:11 (МСК) · текст от 29.09.2026, https://…/consent/ · IP …» («текст согласия ещё не опубликован» while the page is a draft; the checkbox word is unlinked then). The page reuses the policy's template and fields (`page-privacy.php`, a second page on it); text drafted in `consent-ru-draft.md` from the policy's table, seeded as a **draft** by `tools/page-seed.php consent` — publishing it is the owner's approval. `[уточнить]` in the draft: the retention term (proposed 3 years from the last message — the author keeps letters so guests can ask about past orders) and the mail services named in the policy (П02). **The policy must follow:** the §3 table's 30-day term and §8.2 for messages, and §4.2's «Оператор сохраняет подтверждение» now means the letter. Author (iPhone, sweetpepper.bar): Брокколи в сухарях showed «С сыром», an empty bullet and «ом». The data was clean («С сыром / С песто с базиликом и грецким орехом»); the splitter was not. `inc/menu-data.php` turned a dish's options into bullets with `preg_split( '/\R/' )` — without the `u` flag PCRE reads bytes, and `\R` counts 0x85 (NEL) as a line break. «х» is D1 85 in UTF-8, so «…оре|х|ом» was cut inside the letter: the first half ended in a lone D1, which `esc_html()` returns as empty — the blank bullet — and «ом» was left. **Fix:** `'/\R/u'`. Any options line with «х» was exposed; today only this one had one.

**The sweep** (author: "check if there are any other bugs with the menu"): every row of `/menu/food/`, `/menu/bar/`, `/en/menu/food/`, `/en/menu/bar/` rendered before and after — 94 + 163 rows a language. The fix changed only the broccoli. No empty or broken text, no markup or entities in the copy, no mixed Latin / Cyrillic words (homoglyphs), no double spaces or spaces before punctuation, no English on the Russian pages or Cyrillic on the English ones, no missing price, no odd portion; in the database every section's lists hold published items only, none twice, no empty sub-list, and the counts match the pages. The other byte-mode regexes in the theme and tools read SVG attributes, URLs and numbers (ASCII) — left as they are. **Test and main sites:** sync `inc/menu-data.php` — code only, no database step.

## Social cards — the copy ground is a band, not a gradient — 28 September 2026

The author tried another body overlay in Figma (`instagram-feed-cards` 238:3699, all six states) and it is built on desktop: the gradient that faded the photo into the copy is gone, and the card's bottom 98px is a **flat translucent band** — Paper 88% by day, Peppercorn 78% by night — edged at the top by a hairline (Peppercorn 18% / Paper 16%) with a soft inner shadow under it (white 24%, 1px 3px by day; black 22%, 1px 4px by night). The photo still reads through it. The date row is Figma's 26px badge row, the title right under it (the 4px gap is gone; the title's position is unchanged because the band packs from the bottom). Pinned is unchanged; the hover is rebuilt below.

- `src/css/events.css` → Body overlay, Date Badge. **Phones take the same band** (author, same day): the three phone gradients (day, night, pinned night) are gone, the hairline and inner shadow come with the band; the phone body stays 82px at 16px padding.
- **Where the band's "extra space" comes from (measured, not padding creep).** Date ink starts 34px under the band's edge, 27px of space under the title: 1px hairline + 24 padding + 4px because Figma's 26px badge row centres an 18px date line + ~5px of Golos leading above the letters. The copy box DevTools shows 76px tall is the waiting CTA, clipped — no visible space. Options shot for the author: B (24px, date row top-aligned + 8px: 30 / 27) and C (16px, band 82 as on phones, pill at 16: 22 / 19). **The author picked B; built:** the date row drops the 26px min-height and takes an 8px margin under it (18.2 + 8 keeps the hover's 26px lift); a card with no date keeps an empty row one caption line tall. Measured: 30 / 27 at rest, 30 / 28 on hover; phones unchanged (they already used 8px). Figma still draws the 26px centred badge row — a design.md §9 debt.

**Day hover: an Avocado stroke** (author: the shadow alone was not prominent enough). By day the event cards and the «Ещё во ВКонтакте» tile turn their Mushroom stroke Avocado on hover and press — the day twin of the night Lime; pinned keeps its Lemon. Scoped to `events.css` (the shared recipe in `components.css` is being edited by another session). Ideas shot for the author alongside: a 2px stroke, the photo leaning in 1.02 (the About guest cards' hover), the band's top line in Avocado, a 4px lift. **The author picked the 2px stroke + the photo leaning in:** the second pixel is an inset line on the card's `::after` (no layout shift; pressed keeps it under the sink shadow), and the photo scales to 1.02 in both themes on the copy's timing (1022ms, Gentle), back to 1 on press (100ms) as on the About guest cards; reduced motion keeps the photo still. Verified by computed styles in the hover and pressed states at 1440, day and night. (Screenshot note: a card whose top sits on the viewport's top edge loses its top stroke in a `captureScreenshot` clip — a capture artifact, not the page.)
- Verified in headless Chrome at 1440 (day and night; computed band 98px, colours and shadow as drawn) and at 402 (unchanged).

**Hover: one stack that re-arranges, not two layers that crossfade.** The prototype's `body-overlay` is a top-packed auto layout (badge 26 · title · link) and the hover variant hides the badge, so Smart Animate *moves* the title up into the badge's place (y 50 → 24) and brings the link up from below the band (y 90 → 48) as it fades in; the badge fades where it is. Timing read from the prototype's reactions: **Smart Animate, Gentle, 1022ms** — the site had 800ms on the flat curve. Built as one `.event-card-copy` stack in `event-card.php` (the title is printed once now, not twice); the moves take `--ease-gentle`, the fades the flat curve (the house rule), `--event-copy-duration: 1022ms`; the shadow and stroke stay on the shared card recipe's 800ms (Highlights shares it). A card with no date keeps an empty 26px row so the title sits where the hover expects it. Keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`) plays the same move; reduced motion gets a 200ms fade only. Sampled per frame at 1440: title 51 → 25 and link 91.3 → 49.3 (Figma's numbers + the 1px hairline), a ~1px spring overshoot at 700ms, settled by 1.1s.

**Phone caption «…» on iPhone.** The published site cut the caption mid-letter while local Chrome showed «…»: the phone title inherited the desktop's `display: -webkit-box` (the line clamp), and WebKit — Safari and every browser on iOS — draws no `text-overflow` ellipsis on that box. The phone title is `display: block` now. Chrome can't show the WebKit bug, so the check is the author's phone after the next deploy.

Version date from the draft's status line (28.09.2026). The map permission (another session's `map-permission.js`, «Настройки карт») exists because loading Google's embed sends the visitor's IP, browser data and Google's cookies to Google abroad — personal data and a cross-border transfer (section 7 of the draft); a click-to-load keeps that from happening until the guest chooses. Dropping it is the owner's / lawyer's call (a Russian map provider changes the transfer question, not the third-party one).

## The guest's language — the browser proposes, a tap is remembered — 28 September 2026

Author: the site showed Russian to everyone; show the right language from the browser / system settings and remember the guest's choice. **Built (`inc/lang.php` → 6, no new file, no build step):** an inline script, first thing in `<head>` (priority −1, before the daypart script), carries the page's two twin URLs. (1) A tap on the EN / RU pill stores `sp-lang` in `localStorage` — from then on that language wins on every entry, including a shared link in the other language. (2) With nothing stored, `navigator.languages` proposes: the first of ru / be / uk / kk / ky gives RU, `en` or no match gives EN (a German-only browser gets English). Nothing is stored for a proposal. (3) If the answer is not the page's language, `location.replace()` goes to the twin (query and hash kept) before anything paints. **Skipped:** back/forward navigation (a redirect there would trap the Back button), crawlers and headless tools by user agent (each URL must stay indexable in its own language — hreflang tells search engines about the twin; headless CDP screenshots are unaffected), the Customizer. Page-cache-safe: the decision is in the browser, the HTML is the same for everyone. **Checked:** `php -l`; the rendered script run in Node against nine cases — EN browser → `/en/about/?menu=drinks`, RU browser stays, de+ru stays RU, fr-only → EN, a stored RU on an EN browser stays, a stored EN on an RU browser → EN, back/forward and Googlebot stay. **In Chrome at sweet-pepper-bar.local** (lang.php copied to the main checkout; a desktop Chrome user agent, three `Accept-Language` sets): `/about/` → `/en/about/` for en-US and de-DE, stays `/about/` for ru-RU; an English browser on `/` → `/en/`, a tap on РУС → `/` with `sp-lang=ru` stored, a later `/en/visit/` → `/visit/`, Back stays where it lands. **Confirmed by the author (28 Sep 2026):** the behaviour works, and the RU list stands — a Kazakh or Ukrainian browser gets Russian. **For the author:** if your Mac/browser is set to English, `/` will now open `/en/` — tap РУС once and it is remembered.

## Viewport foot, third pass — a static strip Safari copies under its bar — 27 September 2026

Author, three more screenshots with the sliver gone: **nothing fixed at the bottom, and the page shows through the bar's glass** (the theme-color / canvas theory of the previous entry was wrong — the bar had been Paper because the sampled sliver's *first* colour was `var(--bg)`, taken once, before the sampler repainted it Parchment); with the Lime notice up, **Lime under the bar**. So Safari 26 extends the colour of the fixed element that touches the viewport's bottom edge, and takes it once. **Built:** a static 4px `.viewport-foot` printed in `footer.php` on every page — Paper by day, Peppercorn by night and on About / Visit / a vacancy (body classes), Peppercorn with `?bar=dark` — shown on touch screens only (`pointer: coarse`; a desktop window has no such bar, on Chrome for iOS it is a hairline above the browser's own bar); no sampling, no flag. The bottom band notice lifts by the same 4px on touch screens (`cookie-notice.css`), so the strip stays the bottom-most element and the bar keeps the page's ground under the Lime band — the look the author asked for. `--foot-h` in main.css is the one number. `theme-color` from the previous entry stays (Chrome for Android, the PWA shell). Verified on the private server with touch emulation: the strip is 4px at the viewport's bottom, Peppercorn by night / on Visit / with `?bar=dark`, Paper by day, the notice's `bottom` 4px; on a desktop window the strip is `display: none` and the notice sits at 0. *To confirm on the author's phone: a night page, a day page, and a page with the notice up.*

**Fourth pass, same evening (author, seven screenshots):** after a scroll the foot is right; at rest there is none (Safari draws its bar over content it was not given), and under the Lime band a 4px dark line showed — Safari had copied the *notice's* Lime under the bar, not the strip's colour. Both from the same cause: the page was never painting under the bar itself, only lending Safari a colour to copy. **`viewport-fit=cover` on the viewport meta (`header.php`)** — `bottom: 0` is the screen's edge now and `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` is the bar's height, so the strip is `4px + inset` and owns the bar's area at rest and scrolled; the bottom band notice sits at `4px + inset` with its own 12 / 16 padding (its inset padding, dead until now, is superseded on touch screens — last rules in `cookie-notice.css`); the footer's, the reserve sheet's and the vacancy bar's inset paddings, written for this and dead without the meta, come alive. `#page` keeps the content off a notch's sides in landscape (`env(safe-area-inset-left/right)`, 0 in portrait); the fixed header and the drawers are not padded for a landscape notch yet — an edge case, noted. Verified on the private server with Chrome's safe-area override: insets 0 → the strip 4px, the notice at 4px; bottom inset 83 → the strip 87px flush with the screen's edge, the notice at 87px with 16px of its own padding, the footer's padding grown by 83. *Whether Safari 26 reports the bar's height in the inset with `viewport-fit=cover` is the phone's to confirm — the earlier `&cover=1` set the meta at runtime, which Safari may not re-read.*

**Fifth pass (author's phone):** it does not — the inset stays 0 with the meta in the HTML, the strip stayed 4px, the fill still came only on scroll, the line under the notice stayed. So on iOS 26 `bottom: 0` is the bar's top while the page is painted under the bar, and the only way to own that area is to run past the layout viewport: the strip now sits at `bottom: calc(-1 * --foot-under)` (160px, more than any bar is tall) with its height grown by the same, so it physically paints under the bar at rest and scrolled; its visible part above the bar is still `--foot-h`; where the page ends above the browser's bar (Chrome for iOS, Android) the overshoot is off-screen, and it adds no scroll room (measured). `viewport-fit=cover` and the inset terms stay — harmless at 0, right wherever a browser does report one. *To confirm on the phone: the dark bar at rest on a night page, and under the Lime band.*

**Confirmed (author's phone, 27 Sep 2026): the foot works on Safari for iPhone at rest and scrolled, under the Lime band too; nothing on desktop, as intended.** Two trims from the same round: Chrome for iOS ends the page above its own bar and showed the strip as a stray line, and an iPad's Safari keeps its address bar at the top — so the strip and the notice lift are gated on **Safari on an iPhone** (`html[data-foot="safari"]`, set by the head script from the user agent: `iPhone` + `Safari` and none of `CriOS FxiOS EdgiOS OPiOS OPT/ DuckDuckGo YaBrowser GSA/`; an in-app web view has no Safari token, an iPad reports itself as a Mac since iPadOS 13) instead of `pointer: coarse`. Checked against seven user agents on the private server: iPhone Safari on, iPhone Chrome / Firefox / an in-app view, iPad Safari, Android Chrome and Mac Safari off.
## The browser's bar takes the page's ground — `theme-color`, the sliver retired — 27 September 2026

Author, from the phone with the sliver on: at rest Safari shows only the 4px line; once the page scrolls the bar fills — **from the document's background**, not from the sliver (a Paper bar under a Parchment section, with the sliver Parchment between: "looks buggy"); a friend's site keeps a black bar under white sections the same way (no `theme-color` in its head — its canvas is black). So the lever is the page's own ground, and the sampled sliver was the wrong instrument: retired (`viewport-foot.js` / `.css` deleted, `?foot=` inert). **Built instead:** `inc/daypart-head.php` prints `<meta name="theme-color">` beside its first-paint theme script — Paper by day, Peppercorn by night, Peppercorn fixed on About / Visit / a vacancy (`data-bar="fixed"`) — and sets it as the theme is decided; `js/bar-color.js` keeps it in step with `data-theme` afterwards (the engine's hourly flip, the bar menu page). Safari 26 fills its bar from the ground either way; `theme-color` is what Chrome for Android and a PWA shell read, and what Safari reads on the versions that honour it. **`?bar=dark`** paints the canvas itself Peppercorn (`html[data-bar="dark"]`, main.css) and the meta with it, so a day page gets a dark bar under a Paper page — the friend's look — to judge on the phone; the trade is a dark overscroll beyond the day header. The bottom-band notice sits above the bar (`bottom: 0` is the bar's top on iOS 26), so the dark bar with the Lime band above it is what the author asked for, with nothing between. Verified on the private server: the meta is `#FCF7E8` on a day page, `#151317` with `?theme=night`, on Visit, About and the bar menu, `#151317` + a Peppercorn canvas with `?bar=dark`; a runtime theme flip moves it and back; no `.viewport-foot` in the page any more.

## Viewport foot — a ground under Safari's floating bar, prototype behind `?foot=` — 27 September 2026

Author (six phone screenshots): Safari 26 runs the page under its glass tab bar, so whatever sits at the fold shows through it — a map card, the next section's headline, the Lime cookie notice; Chrome for iOS ends the page above its own bar (rounded corners, solid), which the author prefers to "glassmorphism". A page cannot reach the browser chrome; it can own the strip beneath it. **Built, behind URL switches** (the house way; no WebKit can be driven from this Mac, so the phone judges): `js/viewport-foot.js` + `css/viewport-foot.css` — a fixed band at the foot of the viewport, z-index 98 (under the notice at 99, the header at 100), pointer-events none, in the ground of the section passing under it. The ground is **sampled**, not typed: on every scroll the element at the band's top edge is asked for the first opaque background up its ancestors, so the band follows the alternating grounds and the day / night theme (measured on the private server: Peppercorn on the home hero, the section's own colour further down, Parchment / Peppercorn on Visit). Switches: `?foot=solid` (the section's ground — the page appears to end above the bar, Chrome's look) · `?foot=glass` (the ground at 70% over a 20px blur — the bar's own material, one tone darker) · `&h=<px>` (the band's height when the browser reports no bottom safe area; without it `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`) · `&cover=1` (adds `viewport-fit=cover` to the viewport meta at runtime — iOS reports the insets only with it; tests whether Safari 26 then names the bar's height; the site's existing `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` paddings are dead without it) · `&tint=1` (keeps `<meta name="theme-color">` at the sampled ground). **To test on the phone, in this order:** `…/?foot=solid&h=90`, then `&h=` up or down until the band meets the bar; then `…/?foot=solid&cover=1` (no `h`) to see whether Safari fills the height itself; then `foot=glass` with the same. The cookie notice sits above the band by z-index but is not lifted by it yet — a `bottom: var(--foot-h)` on the notice is the follow-up once a height is known (the notice is the other session's). Not built: Chrome's rounded corners on the page's foot — the browser's chrome, not the page's; a radius on the band's top corners would be the imitation, to weigh after the height is settled.

**The phone's answer, same evening (author, three screenshots):** Safari 26 anchors `bottom: 0` at the *top* of its bar and extends the page's bottom-most colour under the bar by itself — with `h=90` a 90px band stood above the bar with Safari's copy beneath (≈190px of fill), with `h=4` the sliver alone gave the Chrome look. `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` reported nothing, `&cover=1` changed nothing. Two faults seen and fixed: (1) the fill was **Paprika** — the sampler had taken the element at the band's top edge, the hero's Reserve button; it now climbs to the first opaque background at least 90% of the viewport wide (a ground, never a button, card or chip) and skips fixed layers (the notice, the header), starting from the first in-flow layer under the point (`elementsFromPoint`), and re-samples on `transitionend` too (a note sliding in or out); (2) **glass reaches the sliver only** — Safari's copy under the bar is solid, so the blur can never cover the bar's area; the switch stays for the record, `solid` is the one that works. Default height now `max(4px, env(safe-area-inset-bottom))`. Verified on the private server: the band stays Peppercorn with the Reserve button at the fold, stays the section's ground while the Lime notice is up at the foot, and follows Visit's grounds. **Follow-up (author):** the notice should sit behind the fill rather than on it — with a 4px sliver that is a `bottom: var(--foot-h)` on the notice, or the band above the notice by z-index; and whether the sliver should become the default without a flag.
